# Non-Degree Programs at Unpad

## Universitas Padjadjaran Student Exchange Program

The Student Exchange Program at Universitas Padjadjaran (Unpad) offers international students an exciting opportunity to study for a semester or a year at one of Indonesia's leading universities. Located in Jatinangor, West Java, Unpad’s main campus is nestled in a serene, green environment that provides an ideal setting for academic and personal growth. The campus is conveniently situated near the vibrant city of Bandung, known for its creative culture and culinary delights.

At Unpad, international students can pursue academic excellence while immersing themselves in Indonesia's rich culture and natural beauty. West Java offers a unique blend of traditional and modern lifestyles, allowing students to experience everything from local arts and crafts to exciting city life. By joining student organizations, cultural events, and community activities, exchange students create unforgettable memories while forging meaningful connections with peers from around the globe.

This program is dedicated to students nominated by an overseas partner university under a bilateral exchange agreement with Unpad. Candidates for the exchange program can apply through the International Office of their home university. Tuition fees are paid to the home university (waived to Unpad); in most cases, the application fee is also waived*.

*Note: The tuition waiver policy of Unpad Student Exchange programs is based on the agreement between Unpad and the partner institution or university, which includes specific notes on the tuition waiver.*

## Unpad International Summer Program (UISP)

### Program Description

The Unpad International Summer Program (UISP) is a ten-day summer course program for international and Indonesian students to embark on a transformative learning experience in Jatinangor, West Java. This program combines academic excellence with cultural immersion, offering participants the opportunity to explore a variety of topics while experiencing Indonesia’s vibrant traditions, rich history, and natural beauty.

This year's program will be conducted from August 4 - 13, 2025, with the main location being in Jatinangor Campus, West Java, Indonesia. The main highlights of the program is that each faculty at Universitas Padjadjaran offers its own distinctive programs, with the focus on unique themes that highlight academic expertise and regional relevance.

### Program Offerings

The 2025 IUSP offers thirteen course programs ranging from science technology and agro-complex topics to health science and humanities and social sciences topics. Students may select three programs based on their interests. Based on their stated priorities and availability, they will be assigned to one program.

### Program Highlights for 2025

*   **Duration:** August 4 - 13, 2025
*   **Location:** Universitas Padjadjaran, West Java
*   **Eligibility:** Open to undergraduate, postgraduate students, and professionals.
*   **Language:** Courses are mainly in English.

### Participation Fee & Inclusions

*   **International students from overseas universities:** 450 USD (early bird: 400 USD, limited spots)
*   **Non-Unpad students (residing in Indonesia):** IDR 6,000,000 (early bird: IDR 5,500,000, limited spots)
*   **Unpad students:** FREE* (for course and program participation)

The fee covers (10 days and 9 nights):
*   Course tuition (USD 150)
*   Accommodation (shared room for two people)
*   Airport pickup and local transportation
*   Meals during the program
*   Cultural activities and excursion
*   Certificate of participation

**Exclusions:** International transportation, visa fees, health insurance, and personal expenses are not included.

**Discount:** Students from Unpad’s partner universities receive a 50% discount on tuition fees (USD 75).

**Note for Unpad students:** Only course tuition and program participation are free. Accommodation and meals are not included and must be covered individually.

### Key Dates for 2025

*   Applicat (Text cut off here)

Application deadline: July 7, 2025
Early bird registration and payment deadline: July 10, 2025
Regular registration and payment deadline: July 21, 2025

**Registration Link**
https://bit.ly/UnpadISP2025

# Unpad International Community Service Program (ICSP)

## Program Overview

The International Community Service Program (ICSP) at Universitas Padjadjaran (Unpad) offers international participants an exceptional opportunity to engage in meaningful service-learning activities in local Indonesian communities. This program focuses on fostering global citizenship, promoting cross-cultural understanding, and addressing real-world challenges through collaboration and community engagement.

The program connects participants with local communities in West Java, where they work alongside community members, local organizations, and Unpad students to contribute to sustainable development initiatives. For this year’s program (August 2025), the grand theme is centered on collaborative, interdisciplinary efforts to empower local communities in West Java, Indonesia.

## Program Highlight

* Duration: 10 days (August 1-11, 2025)
* Location: West Java Province
* Eligibility: Open to undergraduate and postgraduate students, professionals, and volunteers from all disciplines and countries.
* Language: English (with support for local language)
* Certificate: Participants will receive a certificate of completion.

## Program Fees and Benefits

* Individual Participation: 350 USD (for 11 days-10 nights)
* Group Participation (min. 10 people): 320 USD

The fees are inclusive of:
* Shared accommodation
* Meals (10x lunch, 10x dinner, 10x breakfast)
* Airport transfer (scheduled pick-up and drop-off)
* Local transportation during the programs
* Community services activities
* Cultural Trips and Excursion
* Certificate of completion

Note: International transportation, visa fees, health insurance, and personal expenses are not included in the fee.

## Registration

* Application deadline: until June 30, 2025
* Registration and payment deadline: until July 14, 2025

## Further Information

international.unpad.ac.id

# Tailored Short-Course Program

In addition to the above-mentioned programs, we offer a short course program designed and tailored to your institution’s needs. For years, we have hosted diverse, tailored short-course programs, including an Overseas Immersion Program with Ngee Ann Polytechnic, Singapore, a three-week immersion program with University Malaysia Terengganu, a one-week immersion program with Universiti Malaya, Malaysia, and many more.

If your institution is interested to have a tailored short-course program (e.g. immersion, internship) in Unpad, please send your inquiries to: international@unpad.ac.id or global.exchange@unpad.ac.id.

# Unpad Visiting Program

The Visiting Program at Universitas Padjadjaran (Unpad) provides international students and researchers an excellent opportunity to engage with our academic community.

## Visiting Student Program

This program allows students worldwide to study for a short term at Unpad. The program aims to facilitate diverse short-term visiting programs outside the regular Unpad Student Exchange Program, including international students whose home institutions do not have an exchange agreement with Unpad, as well as an international internship program.

## Visiting Researcher Program

This program allows international scholars and researchers to collaborate with Unpad faculty on ongoing research projects or develop their research in partnership with Unpad’s academics. It promotes the exchange of knowledge, innovation, and best practices across academic disciplines. For example, some faculties have international research collaborations with universities in Malaysia (UTM Johor, UMS Sabar, UKM Kelanten), Japanese universities, and others.

# Scholarships for International Student Programs

## Darmasiswa Scholarship

DARMASISWA is a one-year non-degree scholarship program offered to all non-Indonesia citizens from countries which Indonesia has diplomatic relations to study Indonesian language (Bahasa Indonesia), arts, music, crafts and other specific subjects at 68 Indonesian higher education institutions (for 2024/2025 academic year). This program was established in 1974 as part of ASEAN initiative, admitting only students from ASEAN. However, in 1976 DARMASISWA was extended further to other countries. DARMASISWA is organized by the Ministry of Education, Culture, Research, and Technology in collaboration with the Ministry of Foreign Affairs of the Republic of Indonesia.

The main objective of the DARMASISWA program is to increase and cultivate interest in Indonesian language and Indonesian culture among citizens from other countries. It also designed to increase mutual understanding and provide stronger cultural bonds between different nations.

For the latest news regarding Darmasiswa Scholarship:
https://darmasiswa.kemdikbud.go.id/

## Kemitraan Negara Berkembang (KNB)

Beasiswa Kemitraan Negara Berkembang, popularly known as KNB Scholarship, is a prestigious scholarship program offered by the government of Republic of Indonesia to selected applicants originating from developing countries. Managed by the Directorate General of Higher Education, Research and Technology, Ministry of Education, Culture, Research and Technology, the scholarship has been disbursed since 2006 to 1,608 recipients from 97 countries.

The role of KNB Scholarship has been central in supporting Indonesian higher education institutions in their endeavor for internationalization. The number of international students is indicative of global visibility of higher education institutions. In support of Indonesian universities’ internationalization and to accommodate the increasing interests from applicants all around the world, the number of scholarships being awarded every year has continued to grow.

The scholarship has also been continuing to expand its partnership with reputable public and private universities in the country. In doing so, the scholarship has been sustaining the quality improvement of Indonesian higher education institutions in order to meet the standards of internationally reputable universities.

For the latest news regarding KNB:
* Application Information
* Study Programs Available for KNB at Unpad
* For Doctoral Program Applicants Find Your Potential Supervisor Here: Unpad Lecturer’s Academic Expertise

## POSSMA - ASEAN

Padjadjaran Overseas Student for Master Program (POSMA-ASEAN) is offered to international students from ASEAN member countries, including Timor Leste, enrolling in a master’s degree program at Unpad in recognition of their academic achievements.

### Registration

Please visit pendaftaran.unpad.ac.id and select ‘International Students Registration’, complete all required information, and upload the required documents.

### Benefit

* Tuition fees for four semesters
* Support for Indonesian Language Program
* Monthly living allowance
* A round-trip international airfare (direct economy class)
* Health insurance during the program
* Limited Stay Permit (ITAS) during the program
* Thesis research fund

### Eligibility

* Brunei Darussalam
* Cambodia
* Lao PDR
* Malaysia
* Myanmar
* Philippines
* Singapore
* Thailand
* Viet Nam
* Timor-Leste

### Requirement

* Be a citizen of an ASEAN country (except Indonesia) and Timor-Leste.
* Have applied and been accepted for admission to the master program (regular, taught in Bahasa Indonesia) for commencement in 2023.
* Hold a bachelor’s degree in related fields.
* Not be sponsored by a scholarship provider external to the Universitas Padjadjaran.
* Have achieved a TOEFL PBT score of at least 450 or TOEFL IBT score of at least 45 or IELTS score of at least 5.0 or TOEIC score of at least 440 or Duolingo English Test score of at least 80. Can use UNPAD English Language Test (ELT) certificate (maximum 2 years), or International TOEFL – (Internet-Based Test) British Council, IALF, and IDP in the form of the International English Language Testing System (IELTS), or Test of English Proficiency (TOEP) from Pusat Layanan Tes Indonesia (PLTI).
* Provide supporting documents required.

## The Indonesian AID Scholarship (TIAS)

Indonesian AID Scholarship (TIAS) offers fully funded scholarships for civil servants and government-nominated professionals from partner countries to pursue diploma, bachelor’s, or master’s degrees at leading Indonesian universities and polytechnics. This initiative aims to support the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) by enhancing human capital and fostering international cooperation.

TIAS is open to applicants from selected priority countries: Azerbaijan, Bangladesh, Cambodia, Colombia, East Timor, Ethiopia, Fiji, Iran, Kazakhstan, Kenya, Laos, Myanmar, Namibia, Nigeria, Pakistan, Papua New Guinea, Saint Lucia, Solomon Islands, Sri Lanka, Suriname, Tanzania, Uzbekistan, Vanuatu, Vietnam, Zimbabwe.

As one of TIAS’s partner universities, Universitas Padjadjaran offers 11 international programs at the undergraduate and graduate levels:

* International Relations (Bachelor)
* Business Administration (Bachelor)
* Accounting (Bachelor)
* Management (Bachelor)
* Economics (Bachelor)
* Islamic Economics (Bachelor)
* Digital Business (Bachelor)
* Communication (Bachelor)
* Political Science [Concentration in International Relations] (Master)
* Public Administration (Master)
* Management (Master)

### Eligibility Criteria

* Citizen of the priority country.
* Recommendation letter from the government of the candidate’s country.
* Age limits: up to 25 years for bachelor’s programs; up to 42 years for master’s programs (as of the year of application).
* Proficiency in English (TOEFL ITP ≥ 477 or IELTS ≥ 5.5) for non-English-speaking countries.

Application Deadline: May 23, 2025

Apply here
For further information, download the guidebook below: TIAS Booklet

## Unpad Through Time

Universitas Padjadjaran (Unpad) was founded on September 11, 1957, in response to the growing demand for accessible, high-quality higher education in West Java. What began as a regional initiative has evolved into one of Indonesia’s most prominent public universities—known for academic rigor, cultural depth, and social relevance.

Initially headquartered in the city of Bandung, Unpad established its main campus in Jatinangor in 1983. Set against the green hills of Sumedang, this campus has grown into a vibrant academic ecosystem spanning 16 faculties and 2 schools, supported by additional campuses in Bandung, Pangandaran, Jakarta, and Garut. These sites reflect the university’s deep commitment to regional engagement while serving as gateways to broader national and international collaboration.

Unpad’s institutional autonomy was elevated in 2014 when it was designated a Legal Entity State University (PTNBH) by presidential regulation. This milestone marked a transition in how the university governs itself—enabling greater flexibility, independence, and global competitiveness across academic, financial, and operational domains. Since then, Unpad has steadily advanced its mission through research-driven innovation, inclusive education, and strategic partnerships that transcend borders.

Over the past decade, the university has undergone a profound transformation. It expanded its digital infrastructure to support hybrid and online learning, deepened its commitment to inclusive access—particularly for students from underserved backgrounds—and elevated its research agenda through interdisciplinary centers of excellence. The university has also launched a bold sustainability vision through its Smart and Green Campus initiative, embedding environmental responsibility into all facets of university life.

Unpad’s global engagement has grown in both depth and breadth. The university has forged international academic alliances across Asia, Australia, Europe, and North America, developing joint degree programs, collaborative research, and student mobility schemes. As a result, it is increasingly recognized as one of Indonesia’s globally connected universities while remaining grounded in the cultural richness of West Java.

In 2024, Unpad designed its Strategic Plan for 2025-2029, reaffirming its commitment to shaping future global leaders, strengthening social and international impact, and expanding its role as a knowledge epicenter in the fields of health, economy, energy, socio-culture, and agrotechnology. The university’s long-term vision toward 2045 aspires to position Unpad not just as a center of learning but as a transformative institution that empowers people, communities, and systems.

Today, Universitas Padjadjaran stands as a forward-looking, inclusive, and globally engaged public university—proudly Indonesian, rooted in Sundanese values, and committed to shaping a better future through education, research, and public service.

## Vision, Mission, & Objectives

### Vision

To become a world-class center for sustainable, inclusive education and knowledge, encouraging global innovation and creating great impact on society.

### Mission

1. Implementing the Tridharma of higher education which is able to meet the demands of the community of users (stakeholders) of higher education services.
2. Providing internationally competitive higher education that is relevant to the needs of stakeholders, contributing to intellectual development and community welfare.
3. Organizing higher education with professionalism and accountability to enhance the university’s reputation.
4. Forming academic individuals who uphold the nobility of local and national culture within the diversity of world cultures.

### Objectives

Unpad has set strategic goals to be achieved in the realization of its 2025-2029 Vision, focusing on three priority strategic objectives and four driving elements. Each component of these strategic goals is interconnected and mutually reinforcing to achieve Unpad’s 2025-2029 Vision, as presented below:

### Unpad's Strategic Vision Diagram

This diagram illustrates the strategic vision of Universitas Padjadjaran (Unpad). At its core, Unpad's primary goal is to be an "Inclusive Hub." This vision is supported by three main pillars shown in the inner circle:

*   Academic Excellence
*   Innovation
*   Being Impactful

These pillars work together to create a university that embodies the guiding characteristics shown on the outer ring: being Inclusive, World Class, and Sustainable. In essence, it shows how these interconnected values drive Unpad's mission.

**Enablers:**

*   Quality Resources
*   Governance and Digital Transformation
*   Business Development and Sustainable Financial Management
*   Branding and Marketing

## UNPAD Identity

### The Symbol of 6 Parts That Have Heraldic Meaning, Namely:

*   **Torch:** The torch is knowledge which is the torch of life, which brings the light of happiness towards nobility.
*   **Kujang:** The kujang is a Sundanese heirloom weapon as a symbol of strength and courage to protect rights and truth, which is the task of science.
*   **Wings:** The wings (depicted with leaves and nails) are a satire on the city of Pakuan which contains the word "paku" which is the center of the greatness of the "Padjadjaran" kingdom and these nail leaves are wings meaning always trying to achieve a higher level or quality.
*   **Wheel:** The wheel is society, which like a wheel always moves around in the process of change and which is the character of Universitas Padjadjaran.
*   **Lotus Flower:** The blooming lotus flower means eternal glory, sacredness for all time, a symbol of culture and education.
*   **Pentagon, a shield in the shape of a pentagon:** The pentagonal shield is Pancasila, the state principle which is the guideline for all efforts and activities of the university for the benefit of the state and nation.

### Symbolic Meaning of Colors

*   **Yellow:** means glory and nobility (base)
*   **Red:** means cleanliness (wheel and torch flame)
*   **White:** means honesty and purity (kujang and lotus)
*   **Black:** means steadfastness, strength, and fortitude (wings)

## Unpad Sites

### BANDUNG: The Urban Gateway

Strategically located in the heart of Bandung, this city campus functions as Unpad’s urban anchor—hosting graduate-level programs, leadership events, and the university’s executive and administrative core. Bandung campus also supports strategic agenda, global collaborations, and university ceremonial events, making it central to Unpad’s academic diplomacy.

### JATINANGOR: The Academic Heart of Unpad

Our largest and most comprehensive campus, located at the foot of Mount Manglayang, Jatinangor is home to 16 faculties and 2 schools. Spread across 1.78 million square meters, this green, self-contained academic city supports the full student experience—from classroom to community engagement. As the University’s innovation and research hub, Jatinangor integrates world-class learning with public service, cultural exploration, and student-driven initiatives.

### JAKARTA: The Capital Connector

Located in Central Jakarta’s Menara Danareksa, our capital-city campus serves as Unpad’s strategic outreach hub. Designed for postgraduate and executive programs, the Jakarta office fosters academic partnerships, stakeholder engagement, and lifelong learning opportunities—bringing Unpad closer to national policy, industries, and professional communities.

### PANGANDARAN: Coastal Innovation & Community Learning

Unpad Pangandaran connects higher education with coastal community development. Established in collaboration with local and provincial government, this campus focuses on applied disciplines like marine science, animal husbandry, and business co-communication. With a commitment to regional empowerment, Pangandaran campus blends modern facilities with an immersive, hands-on learning environment.

### GARUT: Regional Health & Human Development Hub

Specialized in early childhood education and regional human resource development, Unpad Garut delivers targeted programs tailored to local needs. Its close ties with health services and government position it as a catalyst for inclusive and sustainable growth in the East Priangan region.

## Faculty of Law ⚖

The Faculty of Law at Unpad is one of the four founding faculties that laid the foundation for the university and has become a core part of its identity in the field of education. This is reflected in the establishment of Unpad’s Main Scientific Pattern (Pola Ilmiah Pokok/PIP), which emphasizes "The Noble Development of Law and the Environment in National Development."

The establishment of the Clinical Law Laboratory also serves as a distinctive feature of the Faculty of Law at Unpad. The faculty remains committed to meeting societal needs by upholding the Three Pillars of Higher Education (Tri Dharma Perguruan Tinggi).

### Study Programs

**Undergraduate**

*   Bachelor’s Degree: Law

**Postgraduate**

*   Master’s Degree: Law, Notarial Law
*   Doctoral Degree: Law

### Legal Clinics

*   Civil Law Clinic
*   Criminal Law Clinic
*   Anti-Corruption Law Clinic
*   Environmental Law Clinic

Website: fh.unpad.ac.id

## Faculty of Economics and Business

The Economics and Business Faculty was established in accordance with Government Regulation no. 37 of September 18, 1957. The Faculty was initially a part of Universitas Merdeka under the Foundation of Universitas Merdeka Bandung, established in 1952, before becoming part of Universitas Padjadjaran five years later under the name Faculty of Economics. In 2011, the name of the faculty was modified in order to make it a management and business school that can compete at an international standard, in line with Unpad’s goal to be a world-class university.

The faculty has now become one of the top faculties for economics and business education in Indonesia. The Faculty offers 18 highly accredited programs ranging from Bachelor Programs to Doctoral programs. In total there are 5 Bachelor’s Degree programs, 6 Master’s Degree programs, and 3 Doctoral programs on offer, differing in fields of study from economics and accounting, to marketing, business, and management. The Faculty also runs an accounting Professional Program, part of the requirements to become a professionally [certified accountant].

licensed accountant in Indonesia.

## Study Programs

### Undergraduate
*   Bachelor’s Degree: Accounting, Economics, Management, Islamic Economics, Digital Business

### Postgraduate
*   Professional Entry Program: Accounting
*   Master’s Degree: Economics, Management Science, Accounting, Applied Economics, Management, Integrated Microfinance Management
*   Doctoral Degree: Economics, Management Science, Accounting

Website: feb.unpad.ac.id

## Faculty of Medicine

The idea to establish The faculty of medicine in Indonesia came first from the then Minister of Health, dr. Lie Kiat Teng, at the 1953 The Indonesian Medical Association (IDI) Congress in Surabaya. With the founding of Universitas Padjadjaran based on Government Regulation Number 37 of 1957, the Faculty of Medicine was established alongside three others, making up the first iteration of Universitas Padjadjaran. Adhering to the development of knowledge and technology in medicine, a postgraduate education program was established in 1979. Then, in 2006, the Faculty opened a doctoral program for medicine which was done in collaboration with Universiti Kebangsaan Malaysia’s Faculty of Medicine, proving its international recognition.

Currently, the Faculty of Medicine is focusing on efforts to develop its programs and facilities within the context of advancing internationalization, strengthening research and innovation, and improving the healthcare of the community, especially in West Java. The main goal for the Faculty is to break through into the top 500 ranking in the Life Sciences and Medicine category of the QS World University Rankings.

## Study Programs

### Undergraduate
*   Bachelor’s Degree: Medicine, Veterinary Medicine

### Postgraduate
*   Professional Entry Program: Doctor, Veterinarian
*   Specialist Programs: Anesthesiology and Intensive Therapy, General Surgery, Neurosurgery, Orthopedics and Traumatology, Forensic Medicine and Medico Legal Studies, Psychiatry, Pediatrics, Internal Medicine, Ophthalmology, Dermatology and Venereology, Neurology, Obstetrics and Gynecology, Anatomical Pathology, Clinical Pathology, Nuclear Medicine, Pediatric Surgery, Urology, Cardiovascular Disease, Physical Medicine and Rehabilitation, Reconstructive Plastic Surgery and Aesthetic Medicine, Primary Care Family Medicine, Otolaryngology, Radiology
*   Subspecialty Programs: Surgery, Anesthesiology and Intensive Therapy, Pediatrics, Obstetrics and Gynecology, Internal Medicine
*   Master’s Degree: Anti Aging and Aesthetic Medicine, Epidemiology, Basic Medical Sciences, Midwifery, Public Health
*   Doctoral Degree: Medicine

*   Immunology Laboratory
*   Molecular Genetics Laboratory
*   Parasitology Laboratory
*   Cell Culture Laboratory
*   Microbiology Laboratory

*   Animal Research Laboratory

## Faculty of Mathematics and Natural Science

Inaugurated by the first president of the Republic of Indonesia, Ir. Soekarno, on 1958 accompanied by a speech titled “The Development of the Natural Sciences throughout the Centuries”. The first iteration of the Faculty operated under the name Fakultas Ilmu Pasti dan Ilmu Alam (FIPIA) and included four disciplines, exact sciences (mathematics), physics, chemistry, and biology. In 1982, the name of the Faculty into the Faculty of Mathematics and Natural Science (FMIPA).

Currently, FMIPA offers programs at both undergraduate and graduate levels, with 9 Bachelor’s Degree programs, 5 Master’s Degree programs, and 2 Doctoral programs. All of the programs offered by the Faculty have been highly accredited by the National Accreditation Body (BAN-PT), with most receiving the *unggul* accreditation, the highest within BAN-PT’s system. The Faculty endeavors to excel in education and research which are internationally recognized and impactful to society. FMIPA continuously promotes research that focuses on downstreaming, the increase of international scientific publications, and the acquisition of Intellectual Property Rights for scientific products.

## Study Programs

### Undergraduate
*   Bachelor’s Degree: Mathematics, Chemistry, Physics, Biology, Statistics, Geophysics, Computer Science, Electrical Engineering, Actuarial Science

### Postgraduate
*   Master’s Degree: Chemistry, Applied Statistics, Mathematics, Biology, Physics
*   Doctoral Degree: Chemistry, Mathematics

## Laboratories
*   Applied Mathematics
*   Pure Mathematics
*   Physical and Inorganic Chemistry
*   Chemical Analysis and Separation
*   Chemical Natural Materials and Synthesis
*   Basic Science Laboratory Materials Physics
*   Instrumentation Physics Laboratory
*   Microbiology
*   Biosystems
*   Molecular Biology
*   Statistical Computing
*   Laboratory of Electronics, Electrical Power and Communication Technology
*   Chemical Applications and Services
*   Energy Physics Laboratory
*   Environmental Biology
*   Taxonomy
*   Geophysics
*   Geophysical Computing and Instrumentation
*   Management Information Systems and Multimedia
*   Robotics, Artificial Intelligence and Digital Imaging (RAID) Laboratory

Website: fmipa.unpad.ac.id

## Faculty of Agriculture

This faculty is highly committed to contributing to the development of the agricultural sector in Indonesia. Initially, the faculty was located at Dipati Ukur Street No.37 Bandung, with the classes being spread all over campus grounds. Later on, the faculty moved to Unpad’s Jatinangor campus in September 1986. The Faculty of Agriculture at Universitas Padjadjaran offers a variety of study programs, including undergraduate and postgraduate programs. The study programs are set to develop the productivity, quality, and efficiency of the nation’s agriculture sector, as well as to support its sustainability. To maintain and improve the quality of education and research, the faculty is currently collaborating with numerous universities and institutions, both public and private, on the national and international stage.

The alumni of the Faculty of Agriculture are proven to not only be successful in the field of agriculture as well. They are also capable of competing in several other fields, as there are plenty of alumni who are entrusted with honorable positions in many public and private institutions.

The Faculty of Agriculture puts particular focus on the sustainable productivity of the agriculture sector, internationally recognized innovations and graduates, as well as partnerships, societal contributions, and excellent management supporting the Faculty’s advancement. Everything is done to fulfill Faperta’s vision in becoming an internationally renowned and societally impactful school of agriculture.

## Study Programs

### Undergraduate
*   Bachelor’s Degree: Agrotechnology, Agribusiness

### Postgraduate
*   Master’s Degree: Agricultural Economics, Soil Science, Agronomy
*   Doctoral Degree: Agricultural Sciences

## Laboratories
*   Plant Tissue Culture
*   Pest and Plant Disease
*   Plant Analysis
*   Soil Science Laboratory

Website: faperta.unpad.ac.id

## Faculty of Dentistry

Dentistry applies student-centered learning methods to its teaching, utilizing a competence-based curriculum per the Standar Kompetensi Dokter Gigi Indonesia (Indonesian Dentist Standard of Competence). Said standard consists of 6 domains, namely professionalism, scientific mastery of medicine and dentistry, general physical and stomatognathic examinations, recovery of the stomatognathic system, communal dental and oral health, and management of dental practices.

The Faculty of Dentistry adopts the 7S (or Seven Smart) Program which integrates the Three Pillars of Higher Education with the activities of FKG’s academics, staff, alumni, and the supporting facilities and technologies utilized. The 7S program itself is built on the values of Smart Governance, Smart Campus, Smart Research and Publication, Smart Lecturer and Employee, Smart Student, Smart Business, and Smart Alumni. The Faculty’s main goal is to be a leading education institution in dentistry specializing in the field of oral and maxillofacial health, supported by excellent human resources, and internationally competitive by 2025.

## Study Programs

### Undergraduate
*   Bachelor’s Degree: Dentistry

### Postgraduate
*   Professional Entry Program: Dentistry
*   Specialist Programs: Prosthodontics, Orthodontics, Pediatric Dentistry, Oral Medicine, Conservative Dentistry, Oral and Maxillofacial Surgery, Periodontics, Dentomaxillofacial Radiology
*   Master’s Degree: Dentistry
*   Doctoral Degree: Dentistry

Website: fkg.unpad.ac.id

## Faculty of Social and Political Sciences

Social and Political Sciences was established on October 13, 1958 as the Faculty of Public and Business Administration. The name of the Faculty then changed on August 12, 1960, based on the Decree of the Minister of Education, Pedagogy, and Culture No. 66971/UU/60, to the Faculty of Social and Politics. After December 8, 1983, the Faculty would go through another name change, designating the Faculty its current name.

The various study programs run by The faculty aim to produce outstanding experts proficient in the fields of social and political sciences. To achieve that, the courses teaching fundamental concepts of the social and political sciences are delivered to all students of the faculty. Meanwhile, there are also courses to hone the students’ professional skills, taught especially to students of each program. The courses also provide theoretical understanding as well as instruct the students on the methodologies of research in social and political science. The students are also equipped with the skills to comprehend the phenomena happening in society through fieldwork courses. Currently, The faculty offers 9 Bachelor’s Degree programs, 8 Master’s Degree programs, and 4 Doctoral Degree programs.

## Study Programs

### Undergraduate
*   Bachelor’s Degree: Public Administration, Business Administration (off-campus Program in Pangandaran), International Relations, Social Welfare, Government Science, Anthropology, Business Administration, Sociology, Political Science

### Postgraduate
*   Master’s Degree: Public Administration, Business Administration, Political Science, Government Science, Anthropology, Sociology, Social Welfare, Public Policy
*   Doctoral Degree: Sociology, Administration, International Relations, Social Welfare

Website: fisip.unpad.ac.id

## Faculty of Cultural Sciences

Originally named the Faculty of Letters (Fakultas Sastra), was founded on November 1, 1958 with the Decree of Yayasan Pembina Universitas Padjadjaran No. 6/FS/531 of October 1,

## Faculty of Cultural Sciences

1958 regarding The Establishment of the Faculty of Letters. Two years later, the Faculty gained a stronger legal status with the decree of Minister of Education, Pedagogy, and Culture No. 66971/UU/60 of August 12th 1960, making the Faculty of Letters the 8th faculty to be established in Unpad. The Faculty of Letters changed its name to the Faculty of Cultural Sciences in 2012, adapting to the growing complexity and intersectionality of the faculty instruction.

This illustrated the qualities of the students and graduates in being able to actively communicate in languages they studied as well as comprehending linguistics, literature, history, and culture. The change allows this faculty to develop a more interdisciplinary, multidisciplinary, and transdisciplinary education and study of culture, utilizing a theoretical framework that is more critical, inclusive, and sensitive towards change. The faculty vision is to become a distinguished institution in conducting world-class higher education by 2026. The faculty commits to producing excellent human resources in the fields of language, literature, culture, and history who are adaptive towards change and intelligent in building an entrepreneurial spirit.

### Study Programs

**Website:** fib.unpad.ac.id

## Faculty of Psychology

Universitas Padjadjaran’s Faculty of Psychology (Fakultas Psikologi, or Fapsi) was officially founded on September 2, 1961 at the Unpad Hall in Bandung, with Kol. dr. Soemantri Hardjoprakoso acting as the first Head of the Faculty. In 1996, Fapsi moved its faculty from Unpad’s Bandung Campus at DipatiUkur to Unpad’s Jatinangor Campus. The Faculty of Psychology is a leading contributor to the science of psychology in Indonesia and a pioneer of psychological research through the development of its laboratories. Apart from conducting educational practices, Unpad Fapsi also focuses on developing and applying practical psychological knowledge and methods in order to contribute to society. Fapsi aims to continue developing and applying the science of psychology for the welfare of mankind. The Faculty’s target is to become an internationally renowned higher education institution in the field of Psychology.

### Study Programs

#### Undergraduate
* Bachelor’s Degree: Psychology

#### Postgraduate
* Professional Entry Program: Psychology
* Master’s Degree: Psychology, Professional Psychology
* Doctoral Degree: Psychology

**Website:** psikologi.unpad.ac.id

## Faculty of Animal Husbandry

Universitas Padjadjaran’s Faculty of Animal Husbandry (Fakultas Peternakan, or Fapet) was pioneered by Prof. Dr. Didi Atmadilaga. On July 27, 1963, the faculty was established with the Decree of the Minister of Higher Education and the Sciences no. 86/63. The faculty was then officially founded on September 1, 1963. Fapet first conducted its teaching and administrative processes at a number of locations in Bandung, including the Unpad Fapet Campus at Dago which was in use since 1969. In 1986, the Faculty of Animal Husbandry became one of the first Unpad faculties to entirely relocate to its current campus in Jatinangor, Sumedang.

The students and faculty members have gained numerous achievements at national and international levels. Fapet’s alumni are spread in many institutions and businesses, both in public and private sectors, pursuing various fields including (but not limited to) entrepreneurship, agribusiness, banking, education, as well as research and career development. The Faculty’s works of research have been published in many national and international journals, presented in national and international seminars, and adopted by various industries. Some of the products have obtained Intellectual Property Rights from the Ministry of Justice and Human Rights of the Republic of Indonesia. The faculty continuously aims to improve the quality of the animal husbandry sector, and human resources, in West Java. In terms of community services, the Faculty has been organizing mentoring sessions, counseling, as well as corporate social responsibility (CSR) programs for the local community, farmers, and stakeholders. Fapet strives to become a faculty committed to excellence and environmental friendliness in the science and technologies of animal husbandry.

### Study Programs

#### Undergraduate
* Bachelor’s Degree: Animal Husbandry, Animal Husbandry (off-campus Program in Pangandaran)

#### Postgraduate
* Master’s Degree: Animal Husbandry
* Doctoral Degree: Animal Husbandry

### Laboratories
* Beef Cattle and Fowl Teaching Farm
* Fowl Test Farm
* Livestock Forage Green Farm
* Livestock Produce Processing Lab
* Mini Feed Mills
* Waste Study and Management Unit
* Biofarming areas

**Website:** peternakan.unpad.ac.id

## Faculty of Communication Sciences

Universitas Padjadjaran’s Faculty of Communication Sciences (Fakultas Ilmu Komunikasi, or Fikom) was founded on September 18, 1960, under the name of Fakultas Djurnalistik dan Publisistik. It was in 1982 that the Faculty started to use the name it would operate under to this day. Unpad Fikom was located in Universitas Padjadjaran’s Bandung Campus, before relocating to its current location as part of Unpad’s Jatinangor Campus.

The Faculty of Communication Sciences continuously upgrades the curriculum based on the students’ needs, and as a consequence of the rapid development of science, technology, social systems, politics and economy. Thus, it consistently seeks for opportunities to build partnerships with potential institutions with resources and access to improvement. In addition, the Faculty of Communication Sciences has received numerous awards, namely “The Best School of Communication” from MIX Marketing Magazine (SWA Media Group) for four consecutive years. The award verifies Fikom’s wide recognition. Unpad Fikom always strives to achieve its goal in becoming a nationally and internationally reputable, socially impactful, and research-based higher education institution in the communication sciences.

### Study Programs

#### Undergraduate
* Bachelor Degree: Communication Science, Communication Science (off-campus Program in Pangandaran), Library and Information Science, Journalism, Communication Management, Public Relations, Television and Film

**Website:** fikom.unpad.ac.id

## Faculty of Nursing

The Faculty of Nursing offers one study program at three levels, namely the undergraduate Nursing Study Program, the Nursing Professional Program, and the Nursing Study Program for a Master’s degree. As the second oldest nursing school in Indonesia, the faculty is committed to produce high quality human resources in its field. The Faculty of Nursing education system employs a Competency-Based Curriculum with the learning models that are applied including group discussions, problem-based learning, project-based learning, and discovery learning. Unpad’s Faculty of Nursing aims to realize its vision of becoming a world-renowned faculty of nursing research and education. The Faculty also aims to positively impact society by improving the level of health and quality of life through holistic, integrated, and sustainable ways in the future.

### Study Programs

* Master’s Degree: Nursing

* Nursing Skills
* VNURS Laboratory

**Website:** fkep.unpad.ac.id

## Faculty of Fisheries and Marine Science

These experts are expected to be catalysts and revolutionize the development of the fishery and marine sub-sectors in Indonesia. In its efforts, Unpad’s Faculty of Fisheries and Marine Science upholds the goal of realizing its vision of becoming a distinguished, internationally competitive, research-oriented faculty in the fields of fisheries and marine science.

The Faculty of Agriculture that was established in 1983. In 2005, the program separated and became its own faculty. Unpad’s Faculty of Agroindustrial Technology continues to strive for progress and the advancement of both its fields of study and the Faculty itself. Faculty of Agroindustrial Technology’s vision is to develop into a higher education institution that is committed towards excellence in both the science and application of agroindustrial technology. # Faculty of Pharmacy

The Faculty of Pharmacy was established on February 19, 1959 as a department under the Faculty of Exact and Natural Science (FIPPA). On October 17, 2006, the department separated from the Faculty of Mathematics and Natural Sciences to stand independently and become the Faculty of Pharmacy.

Currently, the faculty offers study programs at four levels, namely a Pharmacy Study Program for a Bachelor’s degree, Professional degree, Master’s degree, and Doctoral Degree, as well as a Master’s Degree in Clinical Pharmacy. All but one of the programs offered by the Faculty have attained the highest level of accreditation per Badan Akreditasi Nasional – Perguruan Tinggi (BAN-PT) or National Accreditation Board for Higher Education.

Unpad’s Faculty of Pharmacy continuously endeavors to establish internationally competitive and relevant higher education practices, apply good governance in its teaching, innovate in pharmaceutical research and development, and carry out collaborations in teaching, research, and community service in accordance with the needs of the people. The Faculty aims to become a world-renowned higher education institution in the field of pharmacy and contribute to the improvement of the quality of public health.

# Faculty of Geological Engineering

The Department officially began its operations on November 17, 1959 under the leadership of its two main founders, Major General Prof. Dr. Moestopo and Drs. M. Koesmono. On December 12, 2007, the Department, which had developed into the Department of Geological Engineering, separated from the Faculty of Mathematics and Natural Sciences to stand alone as the Faculty of Geological Engineering.

The Faculty strives to produce reliable and professional geologists who excel and are capable to compete at national and international levels, specializing in the fields of energy and mineral exploration, geological engineering, and many more. The Faculty seeks to push exemplary research to be published nationally and internationally by prioritizing studies conducted by both professors and students. The results of which are expected to be useful to society. The Faculty of Geological Engineering has become the main information center for geology, especially around West Java and Banten. Thus, the Faculty runs its programs by focusing on a few aspects of its graduates. These range from expertise, published works, balance between learning and research, as well as community service.

Unpad’s Faculty of Geological Engineering upholds its vision to become an international quality institution of Geological Engineering education. Faculty of Geological Engineering Unpad also aims to benefit society greatly, for West Java, Indonesia, and the world.

# PostGraduate School

As a center for the development of multidisciplinary knowledge, the Graduate School of Unpad offers study programs designed to produce graduates who are competent, innovative, and globally competitive.

Based at the Jatinangor campus, the Graduate School of Unpad is supported by qualified teaching staff, modern research facilities, and a network of international collaborations. With an approach that integrates theory, research, and practical application, the school is committed to making a tangible contribution to solving problems at the local, national, and global levels.

# Vocational School

The Vocational School of Universitas Padjadjaran (Unpad) is a higher education institution that focuses on the development of applied skills through practical and innovative approaches. Established to meet the needs for job-ready professionals, the Vocational School of Unpad offers various applied undergraduate programs in fields relevant to industry, such as technology, health, business, and tourism.

The Vocational School is supported by modern facilities, experienced teaching staff, and partnerships with the industrial sector. The curriculum is designed to integrate theory and practice, enabling graduates to compete in both national and international job markets. ## Teaching and Research

Digital transformation in higher education has become an urgent need in the era of the Industrial Revolution 4.0. The learning process must go beyond the limits of space and time, and adopt technology to create a flexible, interactive, and relevant learning experience in line with current developments.

As part of this transformation effort, the Digitization of Learning Materials and Development of an Integrated Learning Management System (LMS) program is designed to create a comprehensive digital ecosystem. This program includes the development of electronic learning materials, digital libraries, and interactive learning modules to support technology-based education that can be accessed by students and lecturers from various locations and platforms.

In addition, this program aims to develop and integrate a learning management system (LMS) that supports various learning models, including online, hybrid, and blended learning. Through this approach, it is expected to create a flexible and interactive learning experience that aligns with modern curriculum needs. This program will strengthen the digital learning platform connected to curriculum demands and the expansion of global access to education.

As part of this ecosystem, Luhung, Universitas Padjadjaran’s non-degree digital learning platform, plays a strategic role in expanding inclusive and lifelong learning. Luhung offers short courses, certifications, and micro-credentials that support reskilling and upskilling for professionals, the public, and students. This non-degree initiative complements formal education by providing accessible, modular, and stackable learning pathways that respond quickly to evolving societal and industry needs.

## Strengthening International Standard Research Quality and Capacity at Unpad

Universitas Padjadjaran is committed to elevating the quality and capacity of its research activities to meet international standards. One of the key priorities is optimizing the rich pool of expertise within its academic community, including active faculty members, retirees, staff, and alumni. A centralized platform will be developed to map and connect these diverse areas of expertise, facilitating cross-disciplinary collaboration and serving as a bridge between academia and industry. This initiative will not only strengthen internal synergy but also showcase Unpad's academic excellence to global stakeholders.

To support this ecosystem, Unpad will invest in state-of-the-art research infrastructure by acquiring cutting-edge equipment and developing comprehensive research databases. These investments will be pursued through creative funding models such as PTN-BH allocations, competitive national and international grants, and partnerships with industry. Furthermore, Unpad aims to boost the commercialization of research outcomes and intellectual property by fostering strategic industrial collaborations, nurturing startups through incubation programs, and reinforcing innovation ecosystems. With these efforts, Unpad envisions a research landscape that contributes meaningfully to economic growth and sustainable development.

## Center of Excellence

### SDGs Center of Universitas Padjadjaran

The SDGs center at Universitas Padjadjaran is a pioneering multidisciplinary research institution, established on May 20, 2016, and recognized as the first independent center in Indonesia dedicated exclusively to supporting the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). The Center aspires to play a strategic role in driving Indonesia’s progress toward the 2030 SDGs targets. As a hub for multidisciplinary research, the SDGs Center fosters academic inquiry on sustainable development across global, national, and local contexts.

It serves as a platform for the dissemination of SDG-related research findings—both from within the university and its external collaborators. Furthermore, the Center is committed to strengthening analytical capacities of academics and policymakers at both national and regional levels through tailored capacity-building programs, enabling more effective formulation and evaluation of SDG-aligned policies.

**Our Vision:** Indonesia achieving SDGs in 2030

**Our Mission:**
*   Providing evidence-based policy recommendations to achieve SDGs
*   Independently monitoring progress of SDGs
*   Mainstreaming SDGs into research and policies
*   Facilitating dialogues to achieve SDGs

**Our Main Activities:**
*   Independent studies
*   Publications
*   Conferences, workshops, seminars
*   Trainings
*   Research collaboration, networking and facilitation

Website: sdgcenter.unpad.ac.id

### Center of Excellence for Pharmaceutical Care Innovation (PHARCI)

The Center of Excellence for Pharmaceutical Care Innovation (PHARCI) was established in 2016 as the Research Center of Pharmaceutical Care Development. PHARCI commits to conduct research, collaborations, capacity developments, and community services to support the implementation of rational drug use and improve the quality of pharmaceutical services.

Our ultimate goal is to improve the quality of life of the community. We believe that by bringing together various scientific disciplines within our team, we can reinforce our efforts to achieve the goals. Our multidisciplinary team comprises experts in the field of clinical pharmacy, pharmacoeconomics, pharmacoepidemiology, drug utilization study, pharmacogenetics, pharmacology, molecular pharmacology, and nutraceuticals. PHARCI has collaborated with various government institutions, industry, health care facilities, NGOs, educational institutions and research centers. These collaborations have created several products, innovations, scientific articles, and policy recommendations.

PHARCI has established several facilities that can support the research and collaboration, such as:
*   Medication and Therapy Adherence Clinics (MTAC)
*   Animal Research and Innovation Center (ARIC)
*   Center of Development for Biologic Therapy
*   Cell Biology and Molecular Laboratory
*   Growth Hub (Business Incubator)

PHARCI also manages two national journals, namely the Indonesian Journal of Clinical Pharmacy (IJCP) and Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacy Research (PCPR), which have been accredited with Sinta 2.

Website: risfarklin.unpad.ac.id

### Center for Digitalization and Development of Sundanese Culture

Lopian Unpad stands for the Center for Digitalization and Development of Sundanese Culture at Universitas Padjadjaran (Unpad). This institution is dedicated to the preservation, documentation, and development of Sundanese culture through digital technologies and scientific research.

**Purpose and Functions**
Lopian Unpad aims to safeguard and promote the rich heritage of Sundanese culture, including ancient manuscripts, audio recordings, books, and other historical documents. Many of these archives are vulnerable to damage or loss. Through digitalization and restoration, Lopian strives to ensure the sustainability of Sundanese cultural heritage, making it accessible for future generations.

**Activities and Programs**
*   Digitalization and Restoration: Converting ancient manuscripts and cultural archives into digital formats for long-term preservation.
*   Scientific Publications: Publishing Lopian: Journal of Local Knowledge, featuring research and scholarly studies on Sundanese culture.
*   Cultural Podcast: Through the Lopian Unpad Podcast, the center explores prominent figures, historical publications, and Sundanese literary works, such as those of Haji Hasan Mustapa and Ajip Rosidi.
*   Courses and Training: offering Sundanese culture courses aimed at enhancing public understanding and appreciation of local cultural heritage.

Website: lopian.unpad.ac.id

### FiNder Unpad (Functional Nano Powder – University Center of Excellence)

Finder Unpad is a leading research center at Universitas Padjadjaran that focuses on the development and application of nanotechnology. Established as a University Center of Excellence (PUI-PT), FiNder integrates multiple disciplines—including physics, chemistry, biology, engineering, and computer science—to foster innovation in nanoscience and provide practical solutions to societal and industrial challenges.

Serving as a bridge between academic research and industrial needs, FiNder Unpad offers a wide range of services, including nanoparticle synthesis, solid and liquid waste treatment, and nanomaterial characterization. Currently, the center provides over 45 specialized services accessible to both academic institutions and private-sector stakeholders. FiNder also actively publishes scholarly research and hosts international conferences such as ICePTI, ICTAP, and the National Physics Symposium (SFN), serving as platforms for scientific collaboration and

...knowledge exchange. FiNder engages in strategic partnerships with various industries, including PT Rekayasa Industri, to support the downstream application of research and the advancement of nano-based technologies in Indonesia. These collaborations reflect FiNder’s mission to strengthen the national innovation ecosystem through interdisciplinary and interinstitutional approaches, contributing to science and technology-based sustainable development.

Equipped with advanced laboratory facilities and supported by a team of expert researchers, FiNder Unpad is committed to becoming a center of excellence in nanotechnology innovation. Through research, training, and technology services, the center not only advances academic development but also contributes meaningfully to social and economic transformation at the national and international levels.

Website: finder.ac.id

## The Center for Environment and Sustainability Science (CESS)

The Center for Environment and Sustainability Science (CESS) at Universitas Padjadjaran (Unpad) is an excellence center established with the aim of advancing knowledge in the fields of environment and sustainability through interdisciplinary and transdisciplinary approaches. CESS plays an important role as a research and development center that not only focuses on environmental studies but also on the social and economic aspects related to sustainability. As the first institution in Indonesia to develop sustainability studies across disciplines, CESS plays a strategic role in addressing global challenges related to climate change, environmental degradation, and food security.

Since its establishment, CESS has actively carried out research programs aimed at solving environmental problems holistically and based on evidence. One of its main goals is to support the achievement of sustainable development that balances ecological, social, and economic interests. CESS is committed to producing research that is not only academically beneficial but also implementable in policies and real actions on the ground.

Various activities carried out by CESS include training and workshops in environmental impact analysis (AMDAL), research on ecosystems, and educational programs to raise awareness about the importance of environmental preservation. One important activity conducted by CESS is water sample collection training, carried out by the Ecology Laboratory of Unpad. Additionally, CESS is also actively participating in international conferences, such as the Global Issues Convention 2024 in Germany, to share knowledge and interdisciplinary research to address global environmental issues.

Moreover, CESS also establishes strong international collaborations with various global institutions and organizations. For example, they visited the FAO headquarters in Rome to discuss urban agriculture initiatives based on climate-resilient cultural heritage. This collaboration demonstrates CESS’s commitment to contributing in developing innovative and adaptive solutions to the environmental challenges faced by global society today.

With an approach based on scientific research and collaboration across disciplines, CESS strives to make a significant contribution to creating a more sustainable society. CESS also encourages the transformation of public policies and development practices that are more environmentally friendly. In this increasingly complex era, CESS is committed to continuing to innovate and have a positive impact in creating a better future for future generations.

Website: cess.unpad.ac.id

## Campus Life

Make the most of your potential at Universitas Padjadjaran.

Campus life at Universitas Padjadjaran was developed with the idea of creating an atmosphere that supports the development of the potential of the entire academic activities—students, lecturers, and educational staff—and Unpad residents both academically and non-academically. To realize this vision, Universitas Padjadjaran has made efforts to maximize the use of various assets across its campuses, including the main campus at Dipati Ukur, Jatinangor, and Pangandaran campus.

To produce outstanding graduates, Universitas Padjadjaran also strives to facilitate students in off-campus learning and extracurricular activities. Through participation in various non-academic activities outside the classroom, students can improve their personal development and also acquire essential soft skills such as emotional intelligence, teamwork, self-management, leadership, creativity, critical thinking, and the ability to solve complex problems.

Universitas Padjadjaran offers a variety of Student Activity Units related to creativity, interests, and talents, including music, sports, arts and culture, community development movements, and more. Students from all academic levels can choose the activity units that interest them and engage in inclusive campus activities. The development of these Student Activity Units is not only aimed at developing creativity, interests, and talents but also at helping students achieve accomplishments both nationally and internationally.

For students who want to live close to the campus, Unpad provides on-campus accommodation. Currently, Unpad accommodates 17 comfortable dormitories, including those in Garut and Pangandaran. Apart from these dormitories, the Unpad campus is also surrounded by lots of affordable rooms or houses for rent and even luxurious apartments. Furthermore, Unpad is equipped with cozy cafeterias or canteens that can serve not only as dining areas but also as study spaces or simply as a place to relax and have little chat with friends.

To help students explore the expansive Jatinangor campus, Unpad provides free on-campus transportation. This service runs continuously from 7 a.m. to 4 p.m., taking passengers from the main gate to all campus facilities. Stops are available at each faculty, where individuals can easily board and disembark.

### Facilities

Some of the facilities at Universitas Padjadjaran used for these activities include:
1. GOR Jati, Universitas Padjadjaran football field;
2. Basketball, volleyball, futsal, and badminton courts at GOR Bale Santika complex;
3. The Secretariat complex for Student Activity Units (UKM) at the university and faculty levels;
4. The fitness center at GOR Jati, Jatinangor

Other than sports facilities and meeting rooms, Universitas Padjadjaran also provides the following facilities for learning outside the classroom:
1. Universitas Padjadjaran Central Library (KANDAGA), operating from 8 a.m. to 10 p.m.
2. Learning Management System (LMS) LiVE Unpad.

## Sustainable and Inclusive Campus

Universitas Padjadjaran is committed to becoming a leading institution in advancing the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) through the development of a sustainable and inclusive campus. This vision is reflected in various environmental initiatives, such as the adoption of renewable energy through solar panel installations, integrated waste management programs, water conservation systems, and the promotion of green transportation including electric shuttles and bike lanes. These efforts aim to reduce the university's environmental footprint while fostering environmental awareness among its academic community.

Social equity and well-being are also central to Unpad’s approach. The university provides scholarships and financial aid for underprivileged students, ensures campus facilities are accessible for persons with disabilities, and offers mental health services through on-campus psychological counseling. These initiatives are designed to create a safe, supportive, and equitable learning environment for all students and staff, regardless of their background.

From an economic perspective, Unpad integrates sustainability into its operations by promoting energy and resource efficiency, digitizing administrative services, and supporting s...

Sustainable entrepreneurship through its innovation and incubation programs. Partnerships with industry and government stakeholders further enable Unpad to align its academic and research agenda with sustainable economic development.

Inclusivity remains a core value in Unpad’s identity. The university actively promotes diversity, gender equality, and accessibility through inclusive learning platforms, anti-discrimination training, and spaces for open dialogue across identities. By embedding sustainability and inclusivity into every layer of its campus life, Unpad aspires to be a model of responsible higher education for Indonesia and beyond.

TOPIC: Hakari Atsuko
CATEGORY: blue archive
STATUS: ACTIVE
CONTENT:
Introduction
One of the four members of Arius Branch School's elite Arius Squad. She's silent and always communicates through sign language, but perhaps there's more to this than meets the eye. Her squadmates call her "princess," and Saori in particular seems very protective of her.

Personality
Atsuko may be very silent, but she has a kind heart. Compared to the rest of the Arius Squad and possibly all of Arius, Atsuko appears to be the kindest, having a personality that can be considered quite exceptional in Arius.

Appearance
Atsuko has lavender colored hair, tied into loose side braids using orange ribbons. Although normally covered by a mask, her eyes are a dark red.

Uniform
Atsuko wears a white dress with long translucent sleeves and a second black inner layer. Underneath her dress, she wears a black turtleneck. Over the dress, she has a black breastplate with the school’s logo and motto in white, as well as four magazine pouches.

On top, Atsuko wears a white hooded jacket with black criss-crossed straps on the left sleeve and a long, loose lavender strap wound around the base of the jacket. On the inner left of the jacket, the school’s motto is embroidered, while the inner right has three black four-point stars attached. On the back is the school’s logo. Wrapped around her upper right arm are two additional, smaller pouches.

For leg wear, she wears white socks tied off at top with an orange ribbon. Over those are black boots with white soles. She has a pistol holster on her left thigh, held up by garter straps on both legs.


Advertisement
Halo
Atsuko's halo is three white crosses in a diagonal with unequal distances, with the center cross being larger than the other two. There are detached bits off the ends of the middle cross.

Firearm
She utilize a CZ Scorpion Evo 3 A1 with an Arius logo decal on the right side of the receiver, and a decal of the school's motto on the left side.

TOPIC: mika misono
CATEGORY: blue archive
STATUS: ACTIVE
CONTENT:
Introduction
A member of Trinity's student council, Tea Party, and one of its three student council presidents. She is the childhood friend of Nagisa and has known her for ten years. She calls her Nagi-chan. Unlike the other two presidents of the Tea Party, she isn't particularly bright when it comes to politics, but her strength is not to be underestimated.

Spoilers!
Personality
She is a very talkative person, rarely particularly paying much mind to the current mood or flow of the conversation. She likes to interject her own, unfiltered thoughts into the current conversation.

Unlike the other two presidents of the Tea Party, she is not particularly bright and can be viewed as a happy-go-lucky type of person. Even in serious situations, she often acts in a carefree manner, though it sometimes devolves into a mockery.

Spoilers!
Appearance
Mika has long pink hair that shifts to a pale blue near the bottom and a bun tied to the right of her head. She has golden eyes. On her back are a pair of angel wings that sprout from around waist level.

Halo
Her halo is comprised of two pink spirals that revolve around a central spherical piece with a ring below it. Three pink sparkles and a number of small blobs float just beyond the sparkles, making her halo resemble a protostar. She's one of the few students with a three-dimensional halo.

Uniform
Mika wears a sleeveless white dress[1] that's frilled near the bottom and is inlaid with a starry print. She wears something that resembles a high-waisted skirt with two pairs of golden buttons, gold trimmings, and blue ribbons over this dress. The area above her bosom is a blue bow that's adorned with a golden cross button accessory on top. Like many members of the Tea Party, she wears a white capelet with golden trimmings.

For legwear, she wears white pantyhose and black high heels with a ribbon on their back.

Mika's bun is adorned with violet flowers. On her left wrist is a scrunchy with the same print as the inside of her dress. Her right wing is decorated with golden objects that hang from purple accessories, all of which resemble celestial bodies.

Firearm
She uses a white Lanchester Mk. I* with a mulled wine furniture. A gold Trinity emblem is imprinted on the stock. A similarly coloured Trinity text is also imprinted on the furniture just below the charging handle.

Universitas  Padjadjaran  Global  Pathways:  International   Programs  2025       Why  Choose  Universitas  Padjadjaran?    Inclusive  Education  Hub:  Welcoming  students  from  diverse  backgrounds.    Top-Ranked  University:  Ranked  among  Indonesia’s  top  universities  with  a  renowned  global   reputation  (QS  Global  Ranking  #596  in  2024).    Strong  Global  Partnerships:  Collaborating  with  universities  worldwide.    Vibrant  Campus  Life:  Featuring  modern  facilities,  innovative  research,  and  a  dynamic   student  community.    Prime  Location:  The  main  campus  is  in  Jatinangor,  Bandung,  a  city  known  for  its  education,   culture,  and  creativity.   International  Undergraduate  Program  (IUP)       Universitas  Padjadjaran  offers  an  International  Undergraduate  Program  (IUP)  designed  to  provide   Indonesian  and  international  students  with  an  opportunity  to  pursue  undergraduate  degrees  in  selected   programs  through  uniquely  designed  learning  methods  that  adhere  to  international  standards.   There  are  two  types  of  IUP:  Global  Excellence  Academy  and  Frontier  Advancement  Program.    Global  Excellence  Academy:  Features  the  chance  for  a  double  degree  program  with  partner   universities,  allowing  students  to  earn  two  degrees  upon  graduation.  Instruction  is  fully  in   English.    Frontier  Advancement  Program:  Offers  a  semester  or  a  year-long  exchange  at  overseas   partner  universities  with  grade  conversions  to  support  academic  continuity.  Instruction  is   bilingual.   Both  use  English  as  the  medium  of  instruction—fully  in  English  for  Global  Excellence  and  bilingually   for  Frontier—and  include  international  guest  lectures,  access  to  global  certifications  (AWS,  LinkedIn   Learning,  Google),  and  practical  learning  approaches. Universitas  Padjadjaran  Postgraduate  Programs       Universitas  Padjadjaran  (Unpad)  offers  a  wide  range  of  postgraduate  programs  designed  to  support   academic  advancement  and  professional  development.  These  programs  are  open  to  both  local  and   international  students  and  are  tailored  to  meet  diverse  academic  and  industry  needs.   Unpad’s  postgraduate  programs  are  open  to  students  from  all  nationalities  and  backgrounds  and   include:    Magister  (Master’s  Programs)    Magister  Berbasis  Project  (Project-Based  Master’s  Programs)    Magister  Double  Degree  (Dual  Degree  Master’s  Programs)    Magister  Rekognisi  Pembelajaran  Lampau  (Master’s  Programs  with  Prior  Learning   Recognition)    Profesi  (Professional  Programs)    Spesialis  dan  Subspesialis  (Specialist  and  Subspecialist  Programs)    Doktor  (Doctoral  Programs)   For  More  Information    Further  information  regarding  Unpad’s  master's  degree  programs  can  be  found  here:   https://smup.unpad.ac.id/magister/    Information  regarding  Unpad’s  doctoral  programs  can  be  found  here:   https://smup.unpad.ac.id/program-doktor/   Joint-Degree  and  Double-Degree  Programs  at  Unpad   Universitas  Padjadjaran  (Unpad)  offers  Joint-Degree  and  Double-Degree  programs  in  collaboration   with  its  esteemed  international  partner  universities.  These  programs  allow  students  to  experience  a   global  academic  environment,  gain  valuable  cross-cultural  insights,  and  graduate  with  prestigious   qualifications  recognized  worldwide.    Joint  Degree  Program:  Offers  an  integrated  academic  experience  where  students  earn  a   single  degree  jointly  awarded  by  Unpad  and  a  partner  university.  This  unique  approach   reflects  the  combined  academic  standards  and  expertise  of  both  institutions.    Double  Degree  Program:  Allows  students  to  study  at  two  universities—Unpad  and  an   international  partner  institution—within  an  integrated  curriculum.  Upon  completion,   participants  receive  degrees  from  both  institutions,  significantly  enhancing  their  academic   and  professional  profile.   Available  Joint-Degree  Collaborations   The  postgraduate  programs  at  Unpad  that  have  collaborations  with  partner  universities  abroad  for   joint  and  double  degree  programs  are  as  follows:    Joint-Degree   Master  of  Psychology  (Science)  with  Universiti  Kebangsaan  Malaysia   Doctor  of  Psychology  with  Dalhousie  University  (Canada)   Doctor  of  Psychology  with  Maastricht  University  (Netherlands)   Obstetrics  and  Gynecology  Specialist  Education  with  Universiti  Kebangsaan   Malaysia   Double  Degree  Programs       
Doctoral  Programs    Doctor  of  Chemistry  with  Graduate  School  of  Pharmaceutical  Sciences,  Graduate  School  of   Medical  and  Pharmaceutical  Sciences,  Chiba  University  (Japan)    Doctor  of  Chemistry  with  Graduate  School  of  Science,  Hokkaido  University  (Japan)    Doctor  of  Pharmacy  with  Graduate  School  of  Pharmaceutical  Sciences,  Chiba  University   (Japan)    Doctor  of  Administrative  Sciences  with  Graduate  School  of  Law,  Tohoku  University   (Japan)   Master's  Programs    Master  of  Applied  Economics  with  Master  of  Public  Management  and  Administration,   Rikkyo  University  (Japan)    Master  of  Applied  Economics  with  Master  of  International  Cooperation,  Takushoku   University  (Japan)    Master  of  Applied  Economics  with  Graduate  Institute  for  Policy  Studies  (GRIPS),  (Japan)    Master  of  Chemistry  with  Graduate  School  of  Science,  Osaka  University  (Japan)    Master  of  Geological  Engineering  with  Graduate  School  of  International  Resource  Sciences,   Akita  University  (Japan)    Master  of  Fisheries  with  Borneo  Marine  Research  Institute,  University  Malaysia  Sabah    Master  of  Marine  Conservation  with  Borneo  Marine  Research  Institute,  University   Malaysia  Sabah    Master  of  Fisheries  with  Master  of  Sciences  in  Environmental  Management,  National   University  of  Singapore    Master  of  Marine  Conservation  with  Master  of  Sciences  in  Environmental  Management,   National  University  of  Singapore    Master  of  Pharmacy  with  Graduate  School  of  Biomedical  Sciences,  Rutgers  University   (United  States)   Non-Degree  Programs  at  Unpad       Universitas  Padjadjaran  Student  Exchange  Program   The  Student  Exchange  Program  at  Universitas  Padjadjaran  (Unpad)  offers  international  students  an   exciting  opportunity  to  study  for  a  semester  or  a  year  at  one  of  Indonesia's  leading  universities.   Located  in  Jatinangor,  West  Java,  Unpad’s  main  campus  is  nestled  in  a  serene,  green  environment  that   provides  an  ideal  setting  for  academic  and  personal  growth.  The  campus  is  conveniently  situated  near   the  vibrant  city  of  Bandung,  known  for  its  creative  culture  and  culinary  delights.   At  Unpad,  international  students  can  pursue  academic  excellence  while  immersing  themselves  in   Indonesia's  rich  culture  and  natural  beauty.  West  Java  offers  a  unique  blend  of  traditional  and  modern   lifestyles,  allowing  students  to  experience  everything  from  local  arts  and  crafts  to  exciting  city  life.  By   joining  student  organizations,  cultural  events,  and  community  activities,  exchange  students  create   unforgettable  memories  while  forging  meaningful  connections  with  peers  from  around  the  globe.   This  program  is  dedicated  to  students  nominated  by  an  overseas  partner  university  under  a  bilateral   exchange  agreement  with  Unpad.  Candidates  for  the  exchange  program  can  apply  through  the   International  Office  of  their  home  university.  Tuition  fees  are  paid  to  the  home  university  (waived   to  Unpad);  in  most  cases,  the  application  fee  is  also  waived*.   Note:  The  tuition  waiver  policy  of  Unpad  Student  Exchange  programs  is  based  on  the  agreement   between  Unpad  and  the  partner  institution  or  university,  which  includes  specific  notes  on  the  tuition   waiver.   Unpad  International  Summer  Program  (UISP)   Program  Description   The  Unpad  International  Summer  Program  (UISP)  is  a  ten-day  summer  course  program  for   international  and  Indonesian  students  to  embark  on  a  transformative  learning  experience  in  Jatinangor,   West  Java.  This  program  combines  academic  excellence  with  cultural  immersion,  offering  participants   the  opportunity  to  explore  a  variety  of  topics  while  experiencing  Indonesia’s  vibrant  traditions,  rich   history,  and  natural  beauty.   
This  year's  program  will  be  conducted  from  August  4  -  13,  2025,  with  the  main  location  being  in   Jatinangor  Campus,  West  Java,  Indonesia.  The  main  highlights  of  the  program  is  that  each  faculty  at   Universitas  Padjadjaran  offers  its  own  distinctive  programs,  with  the  focus  on  unique  themes  that   highlight  academic  expertise  and  regional  relevance.   Program  Offerings   The  2025  IUSP  offers  thirteen  course  programs  ranging  from  science  technology  and  agro-complex   topics  to  health  science  and  humanities  and  social  sciences  topics.  Students  may  select  three  programs   based  on  their  interests.  Based  on  their  stated  priorities  and  availability,  they  will  be  assigned  to  one   program.   Program  Highlights  for  2025    Duration:  August  4  -  13,  2025    Location:  Universitas  Padjadjaran,  West  Java    Eligibility:  Open  to  undergraduate,  postgraduate  students,  and  professionals. This  program  focuses  on  fostering  global  citizenship,   promoting  cross-cultural  understanding,  and  addressing  real-world  challenges  through  collaboration   and  community  engagement.   The  program  connects  participants  with  local  communities  in  West  Java,  where  they  work  alongside   community  members,  local  organizations,  and  Unpad  students  to  contribute  to  sustainable   development  initiatives.  For  this  year’s  program  (August  2025),  the  grand  theme  is  centered  on   collaborative,  interdisciplinary  efforts  to  empower  local  communities  in  West  Java,  Indonesia.
