Unpad  Visiting  Program   The  Visiting  Program  at  Universitas  Padjadjaran  (Unpad)  provides  international  students  and   researchers  an  excellent  opportunity  to  engage  with  our  academic  community.   Visiting  Student  Program   This  program  allows  students  worldwide  to  study  for  a  short  term  at  Unpad.  The  program  aims  to   facilitate  diverse  short-term  visiting  programs  outside  the  regular  Unpad  Student  Exchange  Program,   including  international  students  whose  home  institutions  do  not  have  an  exchange  agreement  with   Unpad,  as  well  as  an  international  internship  program.   Visiting  Researcher  Program   This  program  allows  international  scholars  and  researchers  to  collaborate  with  Unpad  faculty  on   ongoing  research  projects  or  develop  their  research  in  partnership  with  Unpad’s  academics.  It   promotes  the  exchange  of  knowledge,  innovation,  and  best  practices  across  academic  disciplines.  For   example,  some  faculties  have  international  research  collaborations  with  universities  in  Malaysia   (UTM  Johor,  UMS  Sabar,  UKM  Kelanten),  Japanese  universities,  and  others.   Scholarships  for  International  Student  Programs       Darmasiswa  Scholarship   DARMASISWA  is  a  one-year  non-degree  scholarship  program  offered  to  all  non-Indonesia  citizens   from  countries  which  Indonesia  has  diplomatic  relations  to  study  Indonesian  language  (Bahasa   Indonesia),  arts,  music,  crafts  and  other  specific  subjects  at  68  Indonesian  higher  education   institutions  (for  2024/2025  academic  year).  This  program  was  established  in  1974  as  part  of  ASEAN   initiative,  admitting  only  students  from  ASEAN.  However,  in  1976  DARMASISWA  was  extended   further  to  other  countries.  DARMASISWA  is  organized  by  the  Ministry  of  Education,  Culture,   Research,  and  Technology  in  collaboration  with  the  Ministry  of  Foreign  Affairs  of  the  Republic  of   Indonesia.   The  main  objective  of  the  DARMASISWA  program  is  to  increase  and  cultivate  interest  in  Indonesian   
language  and  Indonesian  culture  among  citizens  from  other  countries.  It  also  designed  to  increase   mutual  understanding  and  provide  stronger  cultural  bonds  between  different  nations.   For  the  latest  news  regarding  Darmasiswa  Scholarship:   https://darmasiswa.kemdikbud.go.id/   Kemitraan  Negara  Berkembang  (KNB)   Beasiswa  Kemitraan  Negara  Berkembang,  popularly  known  as  KNB  Scholarship,  is  a  prestigious   scholarship  program  offered  by  the  government  of  Republic  of  Indonesia  to  selected  applicants   originating  from  developing  countries.  Managed  by  the  Directorate  General  of  Higher  Education,   Research  and  Technology,  Ministry  of  Education,  Culture,  Research  and  Technology,  the  scholarship   has  been  disbursed  since  2006  to  1,608  recipients  from  97  countries.   The  role  of  KNB  Scholarship  has  been  central  in  supporting  Indonesian  higher  education  institutions   in  their  endeavor  for  internationalization.  The  number  of  international  students  is  indicative  of  global   visibility  of  higher  education  institutions.  In  support  of  Indonesian  universities’  internationalization   and  to  accommodate  the  increasing  interests  from  applicants  all  around  the  world,  the  number  of   scholarships  being  awarded  every  year  has  continued  to  grow.   The  scholarship  has  also  been  continuing  to  expand  its  partnership  with  reputable  public  and  private   universities  in  the  country.  In  doing  so,  the  scholarship  has  been  sustaining  the  quality  improvement   of  Indonesian  higher  education  institutions  in  order  to  meet  the  standards  of  internationally  reputable   universities. The  Indonesian  AID  Scholarship  (TIAS)   Indonesian  AID  Scholarship  (TIAS)  offers  fully  funded  scholarships  for  civil  servants  and   government-nominated  professionals  from  partner  countries  to  pursue  diploma,  bachelor’s,  or   master’s  degrees  at  leading  Indonesian  universities  and  polytechnics.  This  initiative  aims  to  support   the  Sustainable  Development  Goals  (SDGs)  by  enhancing  human  capital  and  fostering  international   cooperation.   TIAS  is  open  to  applicants  from  selected  priority  countries:  Azerbaijan,  Bangladesh,  Cambodia,   Colombia,  East  Timor,  Ethiopia,  Fiji,  Iran,  Kazakhstan,  Kenya,  Laos,  Myanmar,  Namibia,  Nigeria,   Pakistan,  Papua  New  Guinea,  Saint  Lucia,  Solomon  Islands,  Sri  Lanka,  Suriname,  Tanzania,   Uzbekistan,  Vanuatu,  Vietnam,  Zimbabwe. Welcome  to  Universitas  Padjadjaran   Thank  you  for  considering  Universitas  Padjadjaran   (Unpad)—one  of  Indonesia’s  foremost  public  universities,   where  excellence  meets  purpose.   At  Unpad,  we  believe  that  a  university  should  do  more  than   educate—it  should  empower.  Through  inclusive  learning,   transformative  innovation,  and  profound  engagement  with   society,  we  prepare  our  students  not  just  for  careers,  but   for  meaningful  impact.   Rooted  in  the  cultural  richness  of  West  Java,  we  are   proudly  Indonesian  and  globally  connected.  Our  commitment   to  world-class  teaching,  research,  and  sustainability  is   matched  by  our  dedication  to  equity,  heritage,  and  the   public  good.   As  we  enter  our  next  strategic  chapter,  we  are  investing  in   what  truly  matters:    An  inclusive  and  dynamic  student  experience    A  strong  culture  of  innovation  and  entrepreneurship    Smart,  green  campuses  designed  for  the  future    And  partnerships  that  transcend  borders  and   disciplines   Whether  you  are  a  prospective  student,  academic   collaborator,  or  global  partner,  we  invite  you  to  discover   what  defines  Unpad:  Innovative.  Inclusive.  Impactful.   We  look  forward  to  welcoming  you  into  our  vibrant  and   forward-looking  community.   Prof.  Arief  S.  Kartasasmita  Rector   Content    Unpad  Through  Time    Vision,  Mission,  Objectives    Unpad  Identity    Unpad  Sites    Faculties  and  Schools  at  Unpad   Faculty  of  Law   Faculty  of  Economics  and  Business   Faculty  of  Medicine   Faculty  of  Mathematics  and  Natural  Science   Faculty  of  Agriculture   Faculty  of  Dentistry   Faculty  of  Social  and  Political  Science   Faculty  of  Cultural  Sciences   Faculty  of  Psychology   Faculty  of  Animal  Husbandry   Faculty  of  Communication  Sciences   Faculty  of  Nursing   Faculty  of  Fisheries  and  Marine  Sciences   Faculty  of  Agroindustrial  Technology   Faculty  of  Pharmacy   Faculty  of  Geological  Engineering   Graduate  School   Vocational  School    International  Program    Research  and  Development    Centre  of  Excellence    Campus  Life   
Student  Organization   Supported  Facilities  for  the  Education    Unpad  Position  in  the  World  University  Ranking    List  of  partners    Contact  Us   Unpad  Through  Time   Universitas  Padjadjaran  (Unpad)  was  founded  on  September  11,  1957,   in  response  to  the  growing  demand  for  accessible,  high-quality   higher  education  in  West  Java.  What  began  as  a  regional  initiative   has  evolved  into  one  of  Indonesia’s  most  prominent  public   universities—known  for  academic  rigor,  cultural  depth,  and  social   relevance.   Initially  headquartered  in  the  city  of  Bandung,  Unpad  established   its  main  campus  in  Jatinangor  in  1983.  Set  against  the  green  hills   of  Sumedang,  this  campus  has  grown  into  a  vibrant  academic  ecosystem   spanning  16  faculties  and  2  schools,  supported  by  additional   campuses  in  Bandung,  Pangandaran,  Jakarta,  and  Garut.  These  sites   reflect  the  university’s  deep  commitment  to  regional  engagement   while  serving  as  gateways  to  broader  national  and  international   collaboration.   Unpad’s  institutional  autonomy  was  elevated  in  2014  when  it  was   designated  a  Legal  Entity  State  University  (PTNBH)  by  presidential   regulation.  This  milestone  marked  a  transition  in  how  the  university   governs  itself—enabling  greater  flexibility,  independence,  and   global  competitiveness  across  academic,  financial,  and  operational   domains.  Since  then,  Unpad  has  steadily  advanced  its  mission  through   research-driven  innovation,  inclusive  education,  and  strategic   partnerships  that  transcend  borders.   Over  the  past  decade,  the  university  has  undergone  a  profound   transformation.  It  expanded  its  digital  infrastructure  to  support   hybrid  and  online  learning,  deepened  its  commitment  to  inclusive   access—particularly  for  students  from  underserved  backgrounds—and   elevated  its  research  agenda  through  interdisciplinary  centers  of   excellence.  The  university  has  also  launched  a  bold  sustainability   vision  through  its  Smart  and  Green  Campus  initiative,  embedding   environmental  responsibility  into  all  facets  of  university  life.   Unpad’s  global  engagement  has  grown  in  both  depth  and  breadth.  The   university  has  forged  international  academic  alliances  across  Asia,   Australia,  Europe,  and  North  America,  developing  joint  degree   programs,  collaborative  research,  and  student  mobility  schemes.  As  a   result,  it  is  increasingly  recognized  as  one  of  Indonesia’s  globally   connected  universities  while  remaining  grounded  in  the  cultural   richness  of  West  Java.   In  2024,  Unpad  designed  its  Strategic  Plan  for  2025-2029,   reaffirming  its  commitment  to  shaping  future  global  leaders,   strengthening  social  and  international  impact,  and  expanding  its   role  as  a  knowledge  epicenter  in  the  fields  of  health,  economy,   energy,  socio-culture,  and  agrotechnology.  The  university’s   long-term  vision  toward  2045  aspires  to  position  Unpad  not  just  as  a   center  of  learning  but  as  a  transformative  institution  that  empowers   people,  communities,  and  systems.   Today,  Universitas  Padjadjaran  stands  as  a  forward-looking,   inclusive,  and  globally  engaged  public  university—proudly   Indonesian,  rooted  in  Sundanese  values,  and  committed  to  shaping  a   better  future  through  education,  research,  and  public  service.   
Vision,  Mission,  &  Objectives   Vision   To  become  a  world-class  center  for  sustainable,  inclusive   education  and  knowledge,  encouraging  global  innovation  and   creating  great  impact  on  society.   Mission   1.  Implementing  the  Tridharma  of  higher  education  which  is  able   to  meet  the  demands  of  the  community  of  users  (stakeholders)   of  higher  education  services.   2.  Providing  internationally  competitive  higher  education  that  is   relevant  to  the  needs  of  stakeholders,  contributing  to   intellectual  development  and  community  welfare.   3.  Organizing  higher  education  with  professionalism  and   accountability  to  enhance  the  university’s  reputation.   4.  Forming  academic  individuals  who  uphold  the  nobility  of  local   and  national  culture  within  the  diversity  of  world  cultures.   Objectives   Unpad  has  set  strategic  goals  to  be  achieved  in  the  realization  of   its  2025-2029  Vision,  focusing  on  three  priority  strategic   objectives  and  four  driving  elements.  Each  component  of  these   strategic  goals  is  interconnected  and  mutually  reinforcing  to   achieve  Unpad’s  2025-2029  Vision,  as  presented  below:   Unpad's  Strategic  Vision  Diagram       This  diagram  illustrates  the  strategic  vision  of  Universitas   Padjadjaran  (Unpad).   At  its  core,  Unpad's  primary  goal  is  to  be  an  "Inclusive  Hub."  This   vision  is  supported  by  three  main  pillars  shown  in  the  inner  circle:    Academic  Excellence    Innovation    Being  Impactful   These  pillars  work  together  to  create  a  university  that  embodies  the   guiding  characteristics  shown  on  the  outer  ring:  being  Inclusive,   World  Class,  and  Sustainable.  In  essence,  it  shows  how  these   interconnected  values  drive  Unpad's  mission.   Enablers:    Quality  Resources    Governance  and  Digital  Transformation    Business  Development  and  Sustainable  Financial  Management    Branding  and  Marketing   UNPAD  Identity   The  Symbol  of  6  Parts  That  Have  Heraldic  Meaning,  Namely:    Torch:  The  torch  is  knowledge  which  is  the  torch  of  life,   which  brings  the  light  of  happiness  towards  nobility.    Kujang:  The  kujang  is  a  Sundanese  heirloom  weapon  as  a  symbol   of  strength  and  courage  to  protect  rights  and  truth,  which  is   the  task  of  science.    Wings:  The  wings  (depicted  with  leaves  and  nails)  are  a  satire   on  the  city  of  Pakuan  which  contains  the  word  "paku"  which  is   the  center  of  the  greatness  of  the  "Padjadjaran"  kingdom  and   these  nail  leaves  are  wings  meaning  always  trying  to  achieve  a   higher  level  or  quality.    Wheel:  The  wheel  is  society,  which  like  a  wheel  always  moves   
around  in  the  process  of  change  and  which  is  the  character  of   Universitas  Padjadjaran.    Lotus  Flower:  The  blooming  lotus  flower  means  eternal  glory,   sacredness  for  all  time,  a  symbol  of  culture  and  education.    Pentagon,  a  shield  in  the  shape  of  a  pentagon:  The  pentagonal   shield  is  Pancasila,  the  state  principle  which  is  the   guideline  for  all  efforts  and  activities  of  the  university  for   the  benefit  of  the  state  and  nation.   Symbolic  Meaning  of  Colors    Yellow:  means  glory  and  nobility  (base)    Red:  means  cleanliness  (wheel  and  torch  flame)    White:  means  honesty  and  purity  (kujang  and  lotus)    Black:  means  steadfastness,  strength,  and  fortitude  (wings)      Unpad  Sites       BANDUNG:  The  Urban  Gateway   Strategically  located  in  the  heart  of  Bandung,  this  city  campus   functions  as  Unpad’s  urban  anchor—hosting  graduate-level  programs,   leadership  events,  and  the  university’s  executive  and  administrative   core.  Bandung  campus  also  supports  strategic  agenda,  global   collaborations,  and  university  ceremonial  events,  making  it  central   to  Unpad’s  academic  diplomacy.   JATINANGOR:  The  Academic  Heart  of  Unpad   Our  largest  and  most  comprehensive  campus,  located  at  the  foot  of   Mount  Manglayang,  Jatinangor  is  home  to  16  faculties  and  2  schools.   Spread  across  1.78  million  square  meters,  this  green,  self-contained   academic  city  supports  the  full  student  experience—from  classroom  to   community  engagement.  As  the  University’s  innovation  and  research   hub,  Jatinangor  integrates  world-class  learning  with  public  service,   cultural  exploration,  and  student-driven  initiatives.   JAKARTA:  The  Capital  Connector   Located  in  Central  Jakarta’s  Menara  Danareksa,  our  capital-city   campus  serves  as  Unpad’s  strategic  outreach  hub.  Designed  for   postgraduate  and  executive  programs,  the  Jakarta  office  fosters   academic  partnerships,  stakeholder  engagement,  and  lifelong  learning   opportunities—bringing  Unpad  closer  to  national  policy,  industries,   and  professional  communities.   PANGANDARAN:  Coastal  Innovation  &  Community  Learning   Unpad  Pangandaran  connects  higher  education  with  coastal  community   development.  Established  in  collaboration  with  local  and  provincial   government,  this  campus  focuses  on  applied  disciplines  like  marine   science,  animal  husbandry,  and  business  co-communication.  With  a   commitment  to  regional  empowerment,  Pangandaran  campus  blends  modern   facilities  with  an  immersive,  hands-on  learning  environment.   GARUT:  Regional  Health  &  Human  Development  Hub   Specialized  in  early  childhood  education  and  regional  human  resource   development,  Unpad  Garut  delivers  targeted  programs  tailored  to   local  needs.  Its  close  ties  with  health  services  and  government   position  it  as  a  catalyst  for  inclusive  and  sustainable  growth  in   the  East  Priangan  region.   Unpad  at  a  Glance

Duration:  4  years   Faculties  and  Schools  at  Unpad    18  Faculties/Schools   Faculty  of  Law   Faculty  of  Economics  and  Business   Faculty  of  Medicine   Faculty  of  Mathematics  and  Natural  Science   Faculty  of  Agriculture   Faculty  of  Dentistry   Faculty  of  Social  and  Political  Science   Faculty  of  Cultural  Sciences   Faculty  of  Psychology   Faculty  of  Animal  Husbandry   Faculty  of  Communication  Sciences   Faculty  of  Nursing   Faculty  of  Fisheries  and  Marine  Sciences   Faculty  of  Agroindustrial  Technology   Faculty  of  Pharmacy   
Faculty  of  Geological  Engineering   Graduate  School   Vocational  School    193  Programs   58  Bachelor’s  Degree  Programs   14  Vocational  Programs   52  Master’s  Degree  Programs   23  Doctoral  Degree  Programs   33  Specialist  Programs   8  Professional  Programs   5  Sub  Specialist  Programs   Faculty  of  Law   ⚖    The  Faculty  of  Law  at  Unpad  is  one  of  the  four  founding  faculties   that  laid  the  foundation  for  the  university  and  has  become  a  core   part  of  its  identity  in  the  field  of  education.  This  is  reflected  in   the  establishment  of  Unpad’s  Main  Scientific  Pattern  (Pola  Ilmiah   Pokok/PIP),  which  emphasizes  "The  Noble  Development  of  Law  and  the   Environment  in  National  Development."   The  establishment  of  the  Clinical  Law  Laboratory  also  serves  as  a   distinctive  feature  of  the  Faculty  of  Law  at  Unpad.  The  faculty   remains  committed  to  meeting  societal  needs  by  upholding  the  Three   Pillars  of  Higher  Education  (Tri  Dharma  Perguruan  Tinggi).   Study  Programs    Undergraduate   Bachelor’s  Degree:  Law    Postgraduate   Master’s  Degree:  Law,  Notarial  Law   Doctoral  Degree:  Law   Legal  Clinics    Civil  Law  Clinic    Criminal  Law  Clinic    Anti-Corruption  Law  Clinic    Environmental  Law  Clinic   Website:  fh.unpad.ac.id   Faculty  of  Economics  and  Business       The  Economics  and  Business  Faculty  was  established  in  accordance   with  Government  Regulation  no.  37  of  September  18,  1957.  The  Faculty   was  initially  a  part  of  Universitas  Merdeka  under  the  Foundation  of   Universitas  Merdeka  Bandung,  established  in  1952,  before  becoming   part  of  Universitas  Padjadjaran  five  years  later  under  the  name   Faculty  of  Economics.  In  2011,  the  name  of  the  faculty  was  modified   in  order  to  make  it  a  management  and  business  school  that  can   compete  at  an  international  standard,  in  line  with  Unpad’s  goal  to   be  a  world-class  university.   The  faculty  has  now  become  one  of  the  top  faculties  for  economics   and  business  education  in  Indonesia.  The  Faculty  offers  18  highly   accredited  programs  ranging  from  Bachelor  Programs  to  Doctoral   programs.  In  total  there  are  5  Bachelor’s  Degree  programs,  6   Master’s  Degree  programs,  and  3  Doctoral  programs  on  offer,   differing  in  fields  of  study  from  economics  and  accounting,  to   
marketing,  business,  and  management.  The  Faculty  also  runs  an   accounting  Professional  Program,  part  of  the  requirements  to  become   a  professionally  licensed  accountant  in  Indonesia.   Study  Programs    Undergraduate   Bachelor’s  Degree:  Accounting,  Economics,  Management,   Islamic  Economics,  Digital  Business    Postgraduate   Professional  Entry  Program:  Accounting   Master’s  Degree:  Economics,  Management  Science,   Accounting,  Applied  Economics,  Management,  Integrated   Microfinance  Management   Doctoral  Degree:  Economics,  Management  Science,   Accounting   Website:  feb.unpad.ac.id   Faculty  of  Medicine       The  idea  to  establish  The  faculty  of  medicine  in  Indonesia  came   first  from  the  then  Minister  of  Health,  dr.  Lie  Kiat  Teng,  at  the   1953  The  Indonesian  Medical  Association  (IDI)  Congress  in  Surabaya.   With  the  founding  of  Universitas  Padjadjaran  based  on  Government   Regulation  Number  37  of  1957,  the  Faculty  of  Medicine  was   established  alongside  three  others,  making  up  the  first  iteration  of   Universitas  Padjadjaran.  Adhering  to  the  development  of  knowledge   and  technology  in  medicine,  a  postgraduate  education  program  was   established  in  1979.  Then,  in  2006,  the  Faculty  opened  a  doctoral   program  for  medicine  which  was  done  in  collaboration  with  Universiti   Kebangsaan  Malaysia’s  Faculty  of  Medicine,  proving  its  international   recognition.   Currently,  the  Faculty  of  Medicine  is  focusing  on  efforts  to  develop   its  programs  and  facilities  within  the  context  of  advancing   internationalization,  strengthening  research  and  innovation,  and   improving  the  healthcare  of  the  community,  especially  in  West  Java.   The  main  goal  for  the  Faculty  is  to  break  through  into  the  top  500   ranking  in  the  Life  Sciences  and  Medicine  category  of  the  QS  World   University  Rankings.   Study  Programs    Undergraduate   Bachelor’s  Degree:  Medicine,  Veterinary  Medicine    Postgraduate   Professional  Entry  Program:  Doctor,  Veterinarian   Specialist  Programs:  Anesthesiology  and  Intensive   Therapy,  General  Surgery,  Neurosurgery,  Orthopedics  and   Traumatology,  Forensic  Medicine  and  Medico  Legal  Studies,   Psychiatry,  Pediatrics,  Internal  Medicine,  Ophthalmology,   Dermatology  and  Venereology,  Neurology,  Obstetrics  and   Gynecology,  Anatomical  Pathology,  Clinical  Pathology,   Nuclear  Medicine,  Pediatric  Surgery,  Urology,   Cardiovascular  Disease,  Physical  Medicine  and   Rehabilitation,  Reconstructive  Plastic  Surgery  and   Aesthetic  Medicine,  Primary  Care  Family  Medicine,   Otolaryngology,  Radiology   Subspecialty  Programs:  Surgery,  Anesthesiology  and

Soekarno,  on  1958  accompanied  by  a  speech  titled  “The  Development  of   the  Natural  Sciences  throughout  the  Centuries”.  The  first  iteration   of  the  Faculty  operated  under  the  name  Fakultas  Ilmu  Pasti  dan  Ilmu   Alam  (FIPIA)  and  included  four  disciplines,  exact  sciences   (mathematics),  physics,  chemistry,  and  biology.  In  1982,  the  name  of   the  Faculty  into  the  Faculty  of  Mathematics  and  Natural  Science   (FMIPA).   Currently,  FMIPA  offers  programs  at  both  undergraduate  and  graduate   levels,  with  9  Bachelor’s  Degree  programs,  5  Master’s  Degree   programs,  and  2  Doctoral  programs.   All  of  the  programs  offered  by  the  Faculty  have  been  highly   accredited  by  the  National  Accreditation  Body  (BAN-PT),  with  most   receiving  the  unggul  accreditation,  the  highest  within  BAN-PT’s   system.  The  Faculty  endeavors  to  excel  in  education  and  research   which  are  internationally  recognized  and  impactful  to  society.  FMIPA   continuously  promotes  research  that  focuses  on  downstreaming,  the   increase  of  international  scientific  publications,  and  the   acquisition  of  Intellectual  Property  Rights  for  scientific  products. Initially,  the  faculty  was   located  at  Dipati  Ukur  Street  No.37  Bandung,  with  the  classes  being   spread  all  over  campus  grounds.  Later  on,  the  faculty  moved  to   Unpad’s  Jatinangor  campus  in  September  1986.  The  Faculty  of   Agriculture  at  Universitas  Padjadjaran  offers  a  variety  of  study   programs,  including  undergraduate  and  postgraduate  programs.  The   study  programs  are  set  to  develop  the  productivity,  quality,  and   efficiency  of  the  nation’s  agriculture  sector,  as  well  as  to  support   its  sustainability.  To  maintain  and  improve  the  quality  of  education   and  research,  the  faculty  is  currently  collaborating  with  numerous   universities  and  institutions,  both  public  and  private,  on  the   national  and  international  stage.   The  alumni  of  the  Faculty  of  Agriculture  are  proven  to  not  only  be   successful  in  the  field  of  agriculture  as  well.  They  are  also   capable  of  competing  in  several  other  fields,  as  there  are  plenty  of   alumni  who  are  entrusted  with  honorable  positions  in  many  public  and   private  institutions.   The  Faculty  of  Agriculture  puts  particular  focus  on  the  sustainable   productivity  of  the  agriculture  sector,  internationally  recognized   innovations  and  graduates,  as  well  as  partnerships,  societal   contributions,  and  excellent  management  supporting  the  Faculty’s   advancement.  Everything  is  done  to  fulfill  Faperta’s  vision  in   becoming  an  internationally  renowned  and  societally  impactful  school   of  agriculture.  Pest  and  Plant  Disease    Plant  Analysis    Soil  Science  Laboratory   Website:  faperta.unpad.ac.id   Faculty  of  Dentistry       Dentistry  applies  student-centered  learning  methods  to  its  teaching,   utilizing  a  competence-based  curriculum  per  the  Standar  Kompetensi   Dokter  Gigi  Indonesia  (Indonesian  Dentist  Standard  of  Competence).   Said  standard  consists  of  6  domains,  namely  professionalism,   scientific  mastery  of  medicine  and  dentistry,  general  physical  and   stomatognathic  examinations,  recovery  of  the  stomatognathic  system,   communal  dental  and  oral  health,  and  management  of  dental  practices.   The  Faculty  of  Dentistry  adopts  the  7S  (or  Seven  Smart)  Program   which  integrates  the  Three  Pillars  of  Higher  Education  with  the   activities  of  FKG’s  academics,  staff,  alumni,  and  the  supporting   facilities  and  technologies  utilized.  The  7S  program  itself  is  built   on  the  values  of  Smart  Governance,  Smart  Campus,  Smart  Research  and   Publication,  Smart  Lecturer  and  Employee,  Smart  Student,  Smart   Business,  and  Smart  Alumni.  The  Faculty’s  main  goal  is  to  be  a   leading  education  institution  in  dentistry  specializing  in  the  field   of  oral  and  maxillofacial  health,  supported  by  excellent  human   resources,  and  internationally  competitive  by  2025. The  name  of  the   Faculty  then  changed  on  August  12,  1960,  based  on  the  Decree  of  the   Minister  of  Education,  Pedagogy,  and  Culture  No.  66971/UU/60,  to  the   Faculty  of  Social  and  Politics.  After  December  8,  1983,  the  Faculty   would  go  through  another  name  change,  designating  the  Faculty  its   current  name.   The  various  study  programs  run  by  The  faculty  aim  to  produce   outstanding  experts  proficient  in  the  fields  of  social  and  political   sciences.  To  achieve  that,  the  courses  teaching  fundamental  concepts   of  the  social  and  political  sciences  are  delivered  to  all  students   of  the  faculty.  Meanwhile,  there  are  also  courses  to  hone  the   students’  professional  skills,  taught  especially  to  students  of  each   program.  The  courses  also  provide  theoretical  understanding  as  well   
as  instruct  the  students  on  the  methodologies  of  research  in  social   and  political  science.  The  students  are  also  equipped  with  the   skills  to  comprehend  the  phenomena  happening  in  society  through   fieldwork  courses.  Currently,  The  faculty  offers  9  Bachelor’s  Degree   programs,  8  Master’s  Degree  programs,  and  4  Doctoral  Degree   programs. Two  years  later,  the   Faculty  gained  a  stronger  legal  status  with  the  decree  of  Minister   of  Education,  Pedagogy,  and  Culture  No.  66971/UU/60  of  August  12th   1960,  making  the  Faculty  of  Letters  the  8th  faculty  to  be   established  in  Unpad.  The  Faculty  of  Letters  changed  its  name  to  the   Faculty  of  Cultural  Sciences  in  2012,  adapting  to  the  growing   complexity  and  intersectionality  of  the  faculty  instruction.  This   illustrated  the  qualities  of  the  students  and  graduates  in  being   able  to  actively  communicate  in  languages  they  studied  as  well  as   comprehending  linguistics,  literature,  history,  and  culture.   The  change  allows  this  faculty  to  develop  a  more  interdisciplinary,   multidisciplinary,  and  transdisciplinary  education  and  study  of   culture,  utilizing  a  theoretical  framework  that  is  more  critical,   inclusive,  and  sensitive  towards  change.  The  faculty  vision  is  to   become  a  distinguished  institution  in  conducting  world-class  higher   education  by  2026.  The  faculty  commits  to  producing  excellent  human   resources  in  the  fields  of  language,  literature,  culture,  and   history  who  are  adaptive  towards  change  and  intelligent  in  building   an  entrepreneurial  spirit. Laboratories    Language  Laboratory    Japanese  Study  Center  Laboratory   Website:  fib.unpad.ac.id   Faculty  of  Psychology       Universitas  Padjadjaran’s  Faculty  of  Psychology  (Fakultas  Psikologi,   or  Fapsi)  was  officially  founded  on  September  2,  1961  at  the  Unpad   Hall  in  Bandung,  with  Kol.  dr.  Soemantri  Hardjoprakoso  acting  as  the   first  Head  of  the  Faculty.  In  1996,  Fapsi  moved  its  faculty  from   Unpad’s  Bandung  Campus  at  DipatiUkur  to  Unpad’s  Jatinangor  Campus.   The  Faculty  of  Psychology  is  a  leading  contributor  to  the  science  of   psychology  in  Indonesia  and  a  pioneer  of  psychological  research   through  the  development  of  its  laboratories.  Apart  from  conducting   educational  practices,  Unpad  Fapsi  also  focuses  on  developing  and   applying  practical  psychological  knowledge  and  methods  in  order  to   contribute  to  society.   Fapsi  aims  to  continue  developing  and  applying  the  science  of   psychology  for  the  welfare  of  mankind.  The  Faculty’s  target  is  to   become  an  internationally  renowned  higher  education  institution  in   the  field  of  Psychology. On   July  27,  1963,  the  faculty  was  established  with  the  Decree  of  the   Minister  of  Higher  Education  and  the  Sciences  no.  86/63.  The  faculty   was  then  officially  founded  on  September  1,  1963.  Fapet  first   conducted  its  teaching  and  administrative  processes  at  a  number  of   locations  in  Bandung,  including  the  Unpad  Fapet  Campus  at  Dago  which   was  in  use  since  1969.  In  1986,  the  Faculty  of  Animal  Husbandry   became  one  of  the  first  Unpad  faculties  to  entirely  relocate  to  its   current  campus  in  Jatinangor,  Sumedang.   The  students  and  faculty  members  have  gained  numerous  achievements   at  national  and  international  levels.  Fapet’s  alumni  are  spread  in   many  institutions  and  businesses,  both  in  public  and  private   sectors,  pursuing  various  fields  including  (but  not  limited  to)   entrepreneurship,  agribusiness,  banking,  education,  as  well  as   research  and  career  development.  The  Faculty’s  works  of  research   have  been  published  in  many  national  and  international  journals,   presented  in  national  and  international  seminars,  and  adopted  by   various  industries.  Some  of  the  products  have  obtained  Intellectual   Property  Rights  from  the  Ministry  of  Justice  and  Human  Rights  of  the   
Republic  of  Indonesia.  The  faculty  continuously  aims  to  improve  the   quality  of  the  animal  husbandry  sector,  and  human  resources,  in  West   Java.  In  terms  of  community  services,  the  Faculty  has  been   organizing  mentoring  sessions,  counseling,  as  well  as  corporate   social  responsibility  (CSR)  programs  for  the  local  community,   farmers,  and  stakeholders.  Fapet  strives  to  become  a  faculty   committed  to  excellence  and  environmental  friendliness  in  the   science  and  technologies  of  animal  husbandry. It   was  in  1982  that  the  Faculty  started  to  use  the  name  it  would   operate  under  to  this  day.  Unpad  Fikom  was  located  in  Universitas   Padjadjaran’s  Bandung  Campus,  before  relocating  to  its  current   location  as  part  of  Unpad’s  Jatinangor  Campus.   The  Faculty  of  Communication  Sciences  continuously  upgrades  the   curriculum  based  on  the  students’  needs,  and  as  a  consequence  of  the   rapid  development  of  science,  technology,  social  systems,  politics   and  economy.  Thus,  it  consistently  seeks  for  opportunities  to  build   partnerships  with  potential  institutions  with  resources  and  access   to  improvement.  In  addition,  the  Faculty  of  Communication  Sciences   has  received  numerous  awards,  namely  “The  Best  School  of   Communication”  from  MIX  Marketing  Magazine  (SWA  Media  Group)  for   four  consecutive  years.  The  award  verifies  Fikom’s  wide  recognition.   Unpad  Fikom  always  strives  to  achieve  its  goal  in  becoming  a   nationally  and  internationally  reputable,  socially  impactful,  and   research-based  higher  education  institution  in  the  communication   sciences. As  the  second  oldest  nursing  school  in  Indonesia,  the   faculty  is  committed  to  produce  high  quality  human  resources  in  its   field. Unpad’s  Faculty  of  Nursing  aims  to  realize  its  vision  of  becoming  a   world-renowned  faculty  of  nursing  research  and  education.  The   Faculty  also  aims  to  positively  impact  society  by  improving  the   level  of  health  and  quality  of  life  through  holistic,  integrated,   and  sustainable  ways  in  the  future.     The  Faculty  of  Pharmacy  was  established  on  February  19,  1959  as  a   department  under  the  Faculty  of  Exact  and  Natural  Science  (FIPPA).   On  October  17,  2006,  the  department  separated  from  the  Faculty  of   Mathematics  and  Natural  Sciences  to  stand  independently  and  become   the  Faculty  of  Pharmacy.   Currently,  the  faculty  offers  study  programs  at  four  levels,  namely   a  Pharmacy  Study  Program  for  a  Bachelor’s  degree,  Professional   degree,  Master’s  degree,  and  Doctoral  Degree,  as  well  as  a  Master’s   Degree  in  Clinical  Pharmacy.  All  but  one  of  the  programs  offered  by   the  Faculty  have  attained  the  highest  level  of  accreditation  per   Badan  Akreditasi  Nasional  –  Perguruan  Tinggi  (BAN-PT)  or  National   Accreditation  Board  for  Higher  Education.   Unpad’s  Faculty  of  Pharmacy  continuously  endeavors  to  establish   internationally  competitive  and  relevant  higher  education  practices,   apply  good  governance  in  its  teaching,  innovate  in  pharmaceutical   research  and  development,  and  carry  out  collaborations  in  teaching,   research,  and  community  service  in  accordance  with  the  needs  of  the   people.  The  Faculty  aims  to  become  a  world-renowned  higher  education   institution  in  the  field  of  pharmacy  and  contribute  to  the   improvement  of  the  quality  of  public  health.   Study  Programs    Undergraduate   Bachelor’s  Degree:  Pharmacy    Postgraduate   Specialist:  Nuclear  Pharmacy   Professional  Entry  Program:  Pharmacist   Master’s  Degree:  Pharmacy,  Clinical  Pharmacy   Doctoral  Degree:  Pharmacy   Laboratories    Analysis  and  Physicochemistry    Synthesis  and  Medicinal  Chemistry    Pharmaceutics    Pharmaceutical  Technology    Natural  Products    Microbiology  And  Biotechnology    Cell  and  Molecular  Biology    Pharmacology    Clinical  Biochemistry  Laboratory   Website:  farmasi.unpad.ac.id   Faculty  of  Geological  Engineering       The  Department  officially  began  its  operations  on  November  17,  1959   under  the  leadership  of  its  two  main  founders,  Major  General  Prof.   Dr.  Moestopo  and  Drs.  M.  Koesmono.  On  December  12,  2007,  the   Department,  which  had  developed  into  the  Department  of  Geological   Engineering,  separated  from  the  Faculty  of  Mathematics  and  Natural   Sciences  to  stand  alone  as  the  Faculty  of  Geological  Engineering.   The  Faculty  strives  to  produce  reliable  and  professional  geologists   who  excel  and  are  capable  to  compete  at  national  and  international   levels,  specializing  in  the  fields  of  energy  and  mineral   
exploration,  geological  engineering,  and  many  more.  The  Faculty   seeks  to  push  exemplary  research  to  be  published  nationally  and   internationally  by  prioritizing  studies  conducted  by  both  professors   and  students.  The  results  of  which  are  expected  to  be  useful  to   society.  The  Faculty  of  Geological  Engineering  has  become  the  main   information  center  for  geology,  especially  around  West  Java  and   Banten.  Thus,  the  Faculty  runs  its  programs  by  focusing  on  a  few   aspects  of  its  graduates.  These  range  from  expertise,  published   works,  balance  between  learning  and  research,  as  well  as  community   service.   Unpad’s  Faculty  of  Geological  Engineering  upholds  its  vision  to   become  an  international  quality  institution  of  Geological   Engineering  education.  Faculty  of  Geological  Engineering  Unpad  also   aims  to  benefit  society  greatly,  for  West  Java,  Indonesia,  and  the   world. As  a  center  for  the  development  of   multidisciplinary  knowledge,  the  Graduate  School  of  Unpad  offers   study  programs  designed  to  produce  graduates  who  are  competent,   innovative,  and  globally  competitive.   Based  at  the  Jatinangor  campus,  the  Graduate  School  of  Unpad  is   supported  by  qualified  teaching  staff,  modern  research  facilities,   and  a  network  of  international  collaborations.  With  an  approach  that   integrates  theory,  research,  and  practical  application,  the  school   is  committed  to  making  a  tangible  contribution  to  solving  problems   at  the  local,  national,  and  global  levels. Established  to   meet  the  needs  for  job-ready  professionals,  the  Vocational  School  of   Unpad  offers  various  applied  undergraduate  programs  in  fields   relevant  to  industry,  such  as  technology,  health,  business,  and   tourism. The   curriculum  is  designed  to  integrate  theory  and  practice,  enabling   graduates  to  compete  in  both  national  and  international  job  markets.   The  Vocational  School  of  Unpad  is  committed  to  contributing  to  the   development  of  excellent  and  competitive  human  resources. with  Graduate  School  of  Law,  Tohoku  University  (Japan)   Website:  unpad.ac.id/en/international-program   Teaching  and  Research       Digital  transformation  in  higher  education  has  become  an  urgent  need  in  the  era  of  the  Industrial   Revolution  4.0.  The  learning  process  must  go  beyond  the  limits  of  space  and  time,  and  adopt   technology  to  create  a  flexible,  interactive,  and  relevant  learning  experience  in  line  with  current   developments.   
As  part  of  this  transformation  effort,  the  Digitization  of  Learning  Materials  and  Development  of  an   Integrated  Learning  Management  System  (LMS)  program  is  designed  to  create  a  comprehensive   digital  ecosystem.  This  program  includes  the  development  of  electronic  learning  materials,  digital   libraries,  and  interactive  learning  modules  to  support  technology-based  education  that  can  be  accessed   by  students  and  lecturers  from  various  locations  and  platforms.   In  addition,  this  program  aims  to  develop  and  integrate  a  learning  management  system  (LMS)  that   supports  various  learning  models,  including  online,  hybrid,  and  blended  learning.  Through  this   approach,  it  is  expected  to  create  a  flexible  and  interactive  learning  experience  that  aligns  with   modern  curriculum  needs.  This  program  will  strengthen  the  digital  learning  platform  connected  to   curriculum  demands  and  the  expansion  of  global  access  to  education.   As  part  of  this  ecosystem,  Luhung,  Universitas  Padjadjaran’s  non-degree  digital  learning  platform,   plays  a  strategic  role  in  expanding  inclusive  and  lifelong  learning.  Luhung  offers  short  courses,   certifications,  and  micro-credentials  that  support  reskilling  and  upskilling  for  professionals,  the  public,   and  students.  This  non-degree  initiative  complements  formal  education  by  providing  accessible,   modular,  and  stackable  learning  pathways  that  respond  quickly  to  evolving  societal  and  industry   needs.   Strengthening  International  Standard  Research  Quality  and   Capacity  at  Unpad   Universitas  Padjadjaran  is  committed  to  elevating  the  quality  and  capacity  of  its  research  activities  to   meet  international  standards.  One  of  the  key  priorities  is  optimizing  the  rich  pool  of  expertise  within   its  academic  community,  including  active  faculty  members,  retirees,  staff,  and  alumni.  A  centralized   platform  will  be  developed  to  map  and  connect  these  diverse  areas  of  expertise,  facilitating   cross-disciplinary  collaboration  and  serving  as  a  bridge  between  academia  and  industry.  This  initiative   will  not  only  strengthen  internal  synergy  but  also  showcase  Unpad's  academic  excellence  to  global   stakeholders.   To  support  this  ecosystem,  Unpad  will  invest  in  state-of-the-art  research  infrastructure  by  acquiring   cutting-edge  equipment  and  developing  comprehensive  research  databases.   These  investments  will  be  pursued  through  creative  funding  models  such  as  PTN-BH  allocations,   competitive  national  and  international  grants,  and  partnerships  with  industry.  Furthermore,  Unpad   aims  to  boost  the  commercialization  of  research  outcomes  and  intellectual  property  by  fostering   strategic  industrial  collaborations,  nurturing  startups  through  incubation  programs,  and  reinforcing   innovation  ecosystems.  With  these  efforts,  Unpad  envisions  a  research  landscape  that  contributes   meaningfully  to  economic  growth  and  sustainable  development.   Center  of  Excellence       SDGs  Center  of  Universitas  Padjadjaran   The  SDGs  center  at  Universitas  Padjadjaran  is  a  pioneering  multidisciplinary  research  institution,   established  on  May  20,  2016,  and  recognized  as  the  first  independent  center  in  Indonesia  dedicated   exclusively  to  supporting  the  achievement  of  the  Sustainable  Development  Goals  (SDGs).  The  Center   aspires  to  play  a  strategic  role  in  driving  Indonesia’s  progress  toward  the  2030  SDGs  targets.  As  a  hub   for  multidisciplinary  research,  the  SDGs  Center  fosters  academic  inquiry  on  sustainable  development   across  global,  national,  and  local  contexts.   It  serves  as  a  platform  for  the  dissemination  of  SDG-related  research  findings—both  from  within  the   university  and  its  external  collaborators.  Furthermore,  the  Center  is  committed  to  strengthening   analytical  capacities  of  academics  and  policymakers  at  both  national  and  regional  levels  through   tailored  capacity-building  programs,  enabling  more  effective  formulation  and  evaluation  of   SDG-aligned  policies.    Our  Vision:  Indonesia  achieving  SDGs  in  2030    Our  Mission:   Providing  evidence-based  policy  recommendations  to  achieve  SDGs   Independently  monitoring  progress  of  SDGs   Mainstreaming  SDGs  into  research  and  policies   Facilitating  dialogues  to  achieve  SDGs   
 Our  Main  Activities:   Independent  studies   Publications   Conferences,  workshops,  seminars   Trainings   Research  collaboration,  networking  and  facilitation   Website:  sdgcenter.unpad.ac.id   Center  of  Excellence  for  Pharmaceutical  Care  Innovation  (PHARCI)   The  Center  of  Excellence  for  Pharmaceutical  Care  Innovation  (PHARCI)  was  established  in  2016  as   the  Research  Center  of  Pharmaceutical  Care  Development.  PHARCI  commits  to  conduct  research,   collaborations,  capacity  developments,  and  community  services  to  support  the  implementation  of   rational  drug  use  and  improve  the  quality  of  pharmaceutical  services.   Our  ultimate  goal  is  to  improve  the  quality  of  life  of  the  community.  We  believe  that  by  bringing   together  various  scientific  disciplines  within  our  team,  we  can  reinforce  our  efforts  to  achieve  the   goals.  Our  multidisciplinary  team  comprises  experts  in  the  field  of  clinical  pharmacy,   pharmacoeconomics,  pharmacoepidemiology,  drug  utilization  study,  pharmacogenetics,   pharmacology,  molecular  pharmacology,  and  nutraceuticals.  PHARCI  has  collaborated  with  various   government  institutions,  industry,  health  care  facilities,  NGOs,  educational  institutions  and  research   centers.  These  collaborations  have  created  several  products,  innovations,  scientific  articles,  and  policy   recommendations.   PHARCI  has  established  several  facilities  that  can  support  the  research  and  collaboration,  such  as   Medication  and  Therapy  Adherence  Clinics  (MTAC),  Animal  Research  and  Innovation  Center   (ARIC),  Center  of  Development  for  Biologic  Therapy,  Cell  Biology  and  Molecular  Laboratory,  and   Growth  Hub  (Business  Incubator).  PHARCI  also  manages  two  national  journals,  namely  the   Indonesian  Journal  of  Clinical  Pharmacy  (IJCP)  and  Pharmacology  and  Clinical  Pharmacy  Research   (PCPR),  which  have  been  accredited  with  Sinta  2.   Website:  risfarklin.unpad.ac.id   Center  for  Digitalization  and  Development  of  Sundanese  Culture   Lopian  Unpad  stands  for  the  Center  for  Digitalization  and  Development  of  Sundanese  Culture  at   Universitas  Padjadjaran  (Unpad).  This  institution  is  dedicated  to  the  preservation,  documentation,  and   development  of  Sundanese  culture  through  digital  technologies  and  scientific  research.   Purpose  and  Functions   Lopian  Unpad  aims  to  safeguard  and  promote  the  rich  heritage  of  Sundanese  culture,  including   ancient  manuscripts,  audio  recordings,  books,  and  other  historical  documents.  Many  of  these  archives   are  vulnerable  to  damage  or  loss.  Through  digitalization  and  restoration,  Lopian  strives  to  ensure  the   sustainability  of  Sundanese  cultural  heritage,  making  it  accessible  for  future  generations.   Activities  and  Programs    Digitalization  and  Restoration:  Converting  ancient  manuscripts  and  cultural  archives  into   digital  formats  for  long-term  preservation.    Scientific  Publications:  Publishing  Lopian:  Journal  of  Local  Knowledge,  featuring  research   and  scholarly  studies  on  Sundanese  culture.    Cultural  Podcast:  Through  the  Lopian  Unpad  Podcast,  the  center  explores  prominent   figures,  historical  publications,  and  Sundanese  literary  works,  such  as  those  of  Haji  Hasan   Mustapa  and  Ajip  Rosidi.    Courses  and  Training:  offering  Sundanese  culture  courses  aimed  at  enhancing  public   understanding  and  appreciation  of  local  cultural  heritage.   Website:  lopian.unpad.ac.id   FiNder  Unpad  (Functional  Nano  Powder  –  University  Center  of  Excellence)   Finder  Unpad  is  a  leading  research  center  at  Universitas  Padjadjaran  that  focuses  on  the  development   and  application  of  nanotechnology.  Established  as  a  University  Center  of  Excellence  (PUI-PT),   FiNder  integrates  multiple  disciplines—including  physics,  chemistry,  biology,  engineering,  and   computer  science—to  foster  innovation  in  nanoscience  and  provide  practical  solutions  to  societal  and   industrial  challenges.   Serving  as  a  bridge  between  academic  research  and  industrial  needs,  FiNder  Unpad  offers  a  wide   range  of  services,  including  nanoparticle  synthesis,  solid  and  liquid  waste  treatment,  and  nanomaterial   
characterization.  Currently,  the  center  provides  over  45  specialized  services  accessible  to  both   academic  institutions  and  private-sector  stakeholders.  FiNder  also  actively  publishes  scholarly   research  and  hosts  international  conferences  such  as  ICePTI,  ICTAP,  and  the  National  Physics   Symposium  (SFN),  serving  as  platforms  for  scientific  collaboration  and  knowledge  exchange.   FiNder  engages  in  strategic  partnerships  with  various  industries,  including  PT  Rekayasa  Industri,  to   support  the  downstream  application  of  research  and  the  advancement  of  nano-based  technologies  in   Indonesia.  These  collaborations  reflect  FiNder’s  mission  to  strengthen  the  national  innovation   ecosystem  through  interdisciplinary  and  interinstitutional  approaches,  contributing  to  science  and   technology-based  sustainable  development.   Equipped  with  advanced  laboratory  facilities  and  supported  by  a  team  of  expert  researchers,  FiNder   Unpad  is  committed  to  becoming  a  center  of  excellence  in  nanotechnology  innovation.  Through   research,  training,  and  technology  services,  the  center  not  only  advances  academic  development  but   also  contributes  meaningfully  to  social  and  economic  transformation  at  the  national  and  international   levels.   Website:  finder.ac.id   The  Center  for  Environment  and  Sustainability  Science  (CESS)   The  Center  for  Environment  and  Sustainability  Science  (CESS)  at  Universitas  Padjadjaran  (Unpad)  is   an  excellence  center  established  with  the  aim  of  advancing  knowledge  in  the  fields  of  environment   and  sustainability  through  interdisciplinary  and  transdisciplinary  approaches.  CESS  plays  an   important  role  as  a  research  and  development  center  that  not  only  focuses  on  environmental  studies   but  also  on  the  social  and  economic  aspects  related  to  sustainability.  As  the  first  institution  in   Indonesia  to  develop  sustainability  studies  across  disciplines,  CESS  plays  a  strategic  role  in   addressing  global  challenges  related  to  climate  change,  environmental  degradation,  and  food  security.   Since  its  establishment,  CESS  has  actively  carried  out  research  programs  aimed  at  solving   environmental  problems  holistically  and  based  on  evidence.  One  of  its  main  goals  is  to  support  the   achievement  of  sustainable  development  that  balances  ecological,  social,  and  economic  interests.   CESS  is  committed  to  producing  research  that  is  not  only  academically  beneficial  but  also   implementable  in  policies  and  real  actions  on  the  ground.   Various  activities  carried  out  by  CESS  include  training  and  workshops  in  environmental  impact   analysis  (AMDAL),  research  on  ecosystems,  and  educational  programs  to  raise  awareness  about  the   importance  of  environmental  preservation.  One  important  activity  conducted  by  CESS  is  water   sample  collection  training,  carried  out  by  the  Ecology  Laboratory  of  Unpad.  Additionally,  CESS  is   also  actively  participating  in  international  conferences,  such  as  the  Global  Issues  Convention  2024  in   Germany,  to  share  knowledge  and  interdisciplinary  research  to  address  global  environmental  issues.   Moreover,  CESS  also  establishes  strong  international  collaborations  with  various  global  institutions   and  organizations.  For  example,  they  visited  the  FAO  headquarters  in  Rome  to  discuss  urban   agriculture  initiatives  based  on  climate-resilient  cultural  heritage.  This  collaboration  demonstrates   CESS’s  commitment  to  contributing  in  developing  innovative  and  adaptive  solutions  to  the   environmental  challenges  faced  by  global  society  today.   With  an  approach  based  on  scientific  research  and  collaboration  across  disciplines,  CESS  strives  to   make  a  significant  contribution  to  creating  a  more  sustainable  society.  CESS  also  encourages  the   transformation  of  public  policies  and  development  practices  that  are  more  environmentally  friendly.  In   this  increasingly  complex  era,  CESS  is  committed  to  continuing  to  innovate  and  have  a  positive   impact  in  creating  a  better  future  for  future  generations.   Website:  cess.unpad.ac.id   Campus  Life       Make  the  most  of  your  potential  at  Universitas  Padjadjaran.   Campus  life  at  Universitas  Padjadjaran  was  developed  with  the  idea  of  creating  an  atmosphere  that   supports  the  development  of  the  potential  of  the  entire  academic  activities—students,  lecturers,  and   educational  staff—and  Unpad  residents  both  academically  and  non-academically.  To  realize  this   vision,  Universitas  Padjadjaran  has  made  efforts  to  maximize  the  use  of  various  assets  across  its   campuses,  including  the  main  campus  at  Dipati  Ukur,  Jatinangor,  and  Pangandaran  campus.   
To  produce  outstanding  graduates,  Universitas  Padjadjaran  also  strives  to  facilitate  students  in   off-campus  learning  and  extracurricular  activities.  Through  participation  in  various  non-academic   activities  outside  the  classroom,  students  can  improve  their  personal  development  and  also  acquire   essential  soft  skills  such  as  emotional  intelligence,  teamwork,  self-management,  leadership,  creativity,   critical  thinking,  and  the  ability  to  solve  complex  problems.   Universitas  Padjadjaran  offers  a  variety  of  Student  Activity  Units  related  to  creativity,  interests,  and   talents,  including  music,  sports,  arts  and  culture,  community  development  movements,  and  more.   Students  from  all  academic  levels  can  choose  the  activity  units  that  interest  them  and  engage  in   inclusive  campus  activities.  The  development  of  these  Student  Activity  Units  is  not  only  aimed  at   developing  creativity,  interests,  and  talents  but  also  at  helping  students  achieve  accomplishments  both   nationally  and  internationally.   For  students  who  want  to  live  close  to  the  campus,  Unpad  provides  on-campus  accommodation.   Currently,  Unpad  accommodates  17  comfortable  dormitories,  including  those  in  Garut  and   Pangandaran.  Apart  from  these  dormitories,  the  Unpad  campus  is  also  surrounded  by  lots  of   affordable  rooms  or  houses  for  rent  and  even  luxurious  apartments.  Furthermore,  Unpad  is  equipped   with  cozy  cafeterias  or  canteens  that  can  serve  not  only  as  dining  areas  but  also  as  study  spaces  or   simply  as  a  place  to  relax  and  have  little  chat  with  friends.   To  help  students  explore  the  expansive  Jatinangor  campus,  Unpad  provides  free  on-campus   transportation.  This  service  runs  continuously  from  7  a.m.  to  4  p.m.,  taking  passengers  from  the  main   gate  to  all  campus  facilities.  Stops  are  available  at  each  faculty,  where  individuals  can  easily  board   and  disembark.   Facilities   Some  of  the  facilities  at  Universitas  Padjadjaran  used  for  these  activities  include:   1.  GOR  Jati,  Universitas  Padjadjaran  football  field;   2.  Basketball,  volleyball,  futsal,  and  badminton  courts  at  GOR  Bale  Santika  complex;   3.  The  Secretariat  complex  for  Student  Activity  Units  (UKM)  at  the  university  and  faculty   levels;   4.  The  fitness  center  at  GOR  Jati,  Jatinangor   Other  than  sports  facilities  and  meeting  rooms,  Universitas  Padjadjaran  also  provides  the  following   facilities  for  learning  outside  the  classroom:   1.  Universitas  Padjadjaran  Central  Library  (KANDAGA),  operating  from  8  a.m.  to  10  p.m.   2.  Learning  Management  System  (LMS)  LiVE  Unpad.   Student  Organizations       In  2025,  Universitas  Padjadjaran  has  37  student  organizations  at  university  level,  consisting  of  35   Student  Activity  Units  (UKM),  the  Student  Representative  Council  (BPM),  and  the  Student  Executive   Board  (BEM).   In  the  past  five  years  (2020-2024),  Universitas  Padjadjaran  has  won  4,337  national  and  international   championships  in  the  fields  of  arts,  sports,  culture,  and  competency  development  (talents  and   creativity). Unpad  General  Hospital   Sustainable  and  Inclusive  Campus       Universitas  Padjadjaran  is  committed  to  becoming  a  leading  institution  in  advancing  the  Sustainable   Development  Goals  (SDGs)  through  the  development  of  a  sustainable  and  inclusive  campus.  This   vision  is  reflected  in  various  environmental  initiatives,  such  as  the  adoption  of  renewable  energy   through  solar  panel  installations,  integrated  waste  management  programs,  water  conservation  systems,   and  the  promotion  of  green  transportation  including  electric  shuttles  and  bike  lanes.  These  efforts  aim   to  reduce  the  university's  environmental  footprint  while  fostering  environmental  awareness  among  its   academic  community.   Social  equity  and  well-being  are  also  central  to  Unpad’s  approach.  The  university  provides   scholarships  and  financial  aid  for  underprivileged  students,  ensures  campus  facilities  are  accessible   for  persons  with  disabilities,  and  offers  mental  health  services  through  on-campus  psychological   counseling.  These  initiatives  are  designed  to  create  a  safe,  supportive,  and  equitable  learning   environment  for  all  students  and  staff,  regardless  of  their  background.   From  an  economic  perspective,  Unpad  integrates  sustainability  into  its  operations  by  promoting   energy  and  resource  efficiency,  digitizing  administrative  services,  and  supporting  sustainable   entrepreneurship  through  its  innovation  and  incubation  programs.  Partnerships  with  industry  and   government  stakeholders  further  enable  Unpad  to  align  its  academic  and  research  agenda  with   sustainable  economic  development.   Inclusivity  remains  a  core  value  in  Unpad’s  identity.  The  university  actively  promotes  diversity,   gender  equality,  and  accessibility  through  inclusive  learning  platforms,  anti-discrimination  training,   and  spaces  for  open  dialogue  across  identities.  By  embedding  sustainability  and  inclusivity  into  every   layer  of  its  campus  life,  Unpad  aspires  to  be  a  model  of  responsible  higher  education  for  Indonesia   and  beyond. TOPIC: why choose Universitas Padjadjaran?
CATEGORY: unpad
STATUS: ACTIVE
CONTENT:
1. Inclusive Education Hub: Welcoming students from diverse backgrounds. 2. Top-Ranked University: Ranked among Indonesia’s top universities with a renowned global reputation (QS Global Ranking #596 in 2024). 3. Strong Global Partnerships: Collaborating with universities worldwide. 4. Vibrant Campus Life: Featuring modern facilities, innovative research, and a dynamic student community. 5. Prime Location: The main campus is in Jatinangor, Bandung, a city known for its education, culture, and creativity.
